1	# Raven — Claude Code Instructions
2	
3	## Pull Request Workflow
4	
5	After creating a PR with `gh pr create`, **always** immediately queue it for auto-merge:
6	
7	```bash
8	gh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --auto --squash
9	```
10	
11	This queues the PR to squash-merge automatically once all CI checks pass. Do not wait for CI — enqueue immediately after creation.
12	
13	## Branch Naming
14	
15	Use `type/descriptor` format:
16	
17	| Type | When |
18	|------|------|
19	| `feat/` | New feature |
20	| `fix/` | Bug fix |
21	| `refactor/` | Code restructure, no behaviour change |
22	| `ci/` | CI/CD changes |
23	| `chore/` | Tooling, deps, config |
24	| `deps/` | Dependency bumps |
25	
26	## Commit Style
27	
28	- **No AI attribution** — do not add `Co-Authored-By:` trailers to any commit message
29	- Commits should appear as authored by the repo owner only
30	
31	## Rules
32	
33	- **Never push directly to `main`** — always use a PR
34	- **Squash merge only** — never regular merge or rebase-merge
35	- **Never use `--no-verify`** — all hooks must pass
36	- **Never amend published commits** — create new commits instead
37	
```

I'll walk you through three decisions one at a time, then write everything.

### Section A — Issue tracker

The "issue tracker" is where issues live for this repo. Skills like `to-issues`, `triage`, `to-prd`, and `qa` need to know whether to call `gh issue create`, write markdown under `.scratch/`, or follow a custom workflow.

Your `origin` points at `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven`, and your existing labels show you already track work in GitHub Issues. Recommended: **GitHub**.

| Will not be actioned | `wontfix` | exists |

### Section C — Domain docs

Some skills (`improve-codebase-architecture`, `diagnose`, `tdd`) read `CONTEXT.md` to learn the project's domain language and `docs/adr/` for past architectural decisions. Neither exists in your repo yet.

Raven looks like a multi-service repo (Go API, Python AI worker, Vue frontend), so a multi-context layout would let each service have its own glossary. A single root `CONTEXT.md` is simpler and is what most repos use.

1	# Issue tracker: GitHub
2	
3	Issues and PRDs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.
4	
5	## Conventions
6	
7	- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title "..." --body "..."`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.
8	- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --comments`, filtering comments by `jq` and also fetching labels.
9	- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.
10	- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body "..."`
11	- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label "..."` / `--remove-label "..."`
12	- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment "..."`
13	
14	Infer the repo from `git remote -v` — `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.
15	
16	## When a skill says "publish to the issue tracker"
17	
18	Create a GitHub issue.
19	
20	## When a skill says "fetch the relevant ticket"
21	
22	Run `gh issue view <number> --comments`.
23

1	# Domain Docs
2	
3	How the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.
4	
5	## Before exploring, read these
6	
7	- **`CONTEXT.md`** at the repo root, or
8	- **`CONTEXT-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists — it points at one `CONTEXT.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.
9	- **`docs/adr/`** — read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.
10	
11	If any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The producer skill (`/grill-with-docs`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.
12	
13	## File structure
14	
15	Single-context repo (most repos):
16	
17	```
18	/
19	├── CONTEXT.md
20	├── docs/adr/
21	│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md
22	│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md
23	└── src/
24	```
25	
26	Multi-context repo (presence of `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the root):
27	
28	```
29	/
30	├── CONTEXT-MAP.md
31	├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions
32	└── src/
33	    ├── ordering/
34	    │   ├── CONTEXT.md
35	    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions
36	    └── billing/
37	        ├── CONTEXT.md
38	        └── docs/adr/
39	```
40	
41	## Use the glossary's vocabulary
42	
43	When your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `CONTEXT.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.
44	
45	If the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal — either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/grill-with-docs`).
46	
47	## Flag ADR conflicts
48	
49	If your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:
50	
51	> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders) — but worth reopening because…_
52	
```

1	# Resilience Layer for Raven Go API — Design
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-05-08
4	**Status:** Approved (awaiting implementation plan)
5	**Owner:** Jobin Lawrance
6	**Target branch:** `feat/resilience-layer` (worktree, from latest `origin/main`)
7	
8	## Context
9	
10	The Raven Go API today has scattered `context.WithTimeout` usage in `internal/middleware/ratelimit.go`, `internal/service/security.go`, `internal/service/billing.go`, and `internal/repository/semantic_cache.go`, but no consistent discipline at the boundaries that matter most:
11	
12	- **AI worker gRPC client** (`internal/grpc/client.go`) — has fault-injection tests in `internal/integration/grpc_fault_test.go`, but no circuit breaker. A stalled Python worker today will pile up goroutines on the API process until OOM.
13	- **`http.Server` in `cmd/api/main.go`** — uses `gin.Default()` with no `ReadTimeout`/`WriteTimeout`/`IdleTimeout`/`ReadHeaderTimeout` set on the underlying `http.Server`. Go's defaults are zero, which means a slow client can hold a connection forever (slowloris class).
14	- **Per-route deadlines** — Gin handlers do not enforce per-route context budgets. A handler that forgets `ctx.WithTimeout` can run indefinitely.
15	- **Asynq handlers** — task handlers do not consistently apply per-task deadlines, so a stuck handler holds a worker slot indefinitely.
16	
17	No `gobreaker` / `failsafe` / `circuitbreaker` imports exist anywhere in production code today.
18	
19	## Goals
20	
21	1. Bound every external call by an explicit deadline.
22	2. Trip a circuit breaker on the AI-worker gRPC client to prevent cascading failure.
23	3. Harden the `http.Server` against slowloris-class hangs.
24	4. Enforce a per-route Gin context deadline so no handler runs unbounded.
25	5. Apply consistent per-task deadlines in Asynq handlers.
26	6. Add a CI compliance gate that fails when new code introduces external calls without context deadlines.
27	
28	## Non-Goals (deferred to follow-up issues)
29	
30	- Bulkhead semaphores (per-dependency goroutine caps).
31	- Retries with exponential backoff on the gRPC client (Asynq already retries handler-level).
32	- A wholesale audit of every outbound HTTP client construction site (LiveKit, SeaweedFS) — only the callsites we touch as part of this milestone get refactored to the new factory. Remaining audit moves to a follow-up.
33	- OpenObserve dashboards for the new metrics.
34	- Changes to the Python AI worker.
35	
36	## Architecture
37	
38	A new package `internal/resilience/` is the single home for resilience primitives. A sibling middleware lives under `internal/middleware/`.
39	
40	### Package layout
41	
42	```
43	internal/resilience/
44	  policy.go        # Policy struct + functional options + constructor (returns error)
45	  policy_test.go   # Table-driven tests for timeout + breaker behaviour
46	  breaker.go       # Adapter over sony/gobreaker; typed ErrCircuitOpen
47	  grpc.go          # UnaryClientInterceptor(*Policy)
48	  grpc_test.go     # Interceptor unit tests
49	  http.go          # RoundTripper decorator + HTTPClient(*Policy) factory
50	
51	internal/middleware/
52	  deadline.go      # Deadline(d time.Duration) gin.HandlerFunc
53	  deadline_test.go # Per-route deadline application
54	```
55	
56	### `Policy` shape
57	
58	```go
59	type Policy struct {
60	    Name              string
61	    Timeout           time.Duration
62	    BreakerThreshold  uint32        // consecutive failures before Open
63	    BreakerCooldown   time.Duration // time in Open before Half-Open probe
64	    BreakerHalfOpenMax uint32       // requests allowed in Half-Open
65	}
66	
67	type Option func(*Policy)
68	
69	func WithTimeout(d time.Duration) Option { ... }
70	func WithBreakerThreshold(n uint32) Option { ... }
71	func WithBreakerCooldown(d time.Duration) Option { ... }
72	
73	func NewPolicy(name string, opts ...Option) (*Policy, error) {
74	    // sensible defaults, validate on construction
75	}
76	```
77	
78	Defaults:
79	
80	| Knob                | Default | Env override                          |
81	|---------------------|---------|---------------------------------------|
82	| Timeout             | 5s      | `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_TIMEOUT`             |
83	| BreakerThreshold    | 5       | `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_BREAKER_THRESHOLD`   |
84	| BreakerCooldown     | 30s     | `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_BREAKER_COOLDOWN`    |
85	| BreakerHalfOpenMax  | 1       | (no env override; spec'd at 1 probe)  |
86	
87	### Typed errors
88	
89	```go
90	var ErrCircuitOpen = errors.New("resilience: circuit breaker open")
91	```
92	
93	`apierror.ErrorHandler` maps `ErrCircuitOpen` to HTTP 503 with `Retry-After`; `context.DeadlineExceeded` continues to map to 504.
94	
95	## Wiring per boundary
96	
97	### 1. AI worker gRPC client (`internal/grpc/client.go`)
98	
99	- `NewClient` signature changes to accept `*resilience.Policy`.
100	- Interceptor is wired via `grpc.WithChainUnaryInterceptor(resilience.UnaryClientInterceptor(policy))`.
101	- The interceptor:
102	  1. Wraps each call with `context.WithTimeout(ctx, policy.Timeout)` if no shorter deadline already set.
103	  2. Submits the call through `gobreaker.Execute`.
104	  3. Maps `gobreaker.ErrOpenState` / `ErrTooManyRequests` to `ErrCircuitOpen`.
105	  4. Treats `codes.OK`, `codes.NotFound`, `codes.InvalidArgument`, `codes.PermissionDenied`, `codes.Unauthenticated` as **non-failures** for breaker accounting (these are caller errors, not server failures).
106	  5. Treats `codes.Unavailable`, `codes.DeadlineExceeded`, `codes.Internal`, `codes.ResourceExhausted` as failures.
107	
108	`internal/grpc/client_test.go` is updated for the new signature.
109	
110	### 2. `http.Server` in `cmd/api/main.go`
111	
112	Replace the bare server construction with explicit timeouts:
113	
114	```go
115	srv := &http.Server{
116	    Addr:              cfg.HTTP.Addr,
117	    Handler:           router,
118	    ReadHeaderTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
119	    ReadTimeout:       30 * time.Second,
120	    WriteTimeout:      60 * time.Second,
121	    IdleTimeout:       120 * time.Second,
122	}
123	```
124	
125	These values are sized for the longest expected legitimate request (file upload at 30s read, streaming chat response at 60s write). Knobs surface through `cfg.HTTP.*` for environment overrides.
126	
127	### 3. Per-route Gin deadline middleware
128	
129	`internal/middleware/deadline.go`:
130	
131	```go
132	func Deadline(d time.Duration) gin.HandlerFunc {
133	    return func(c *gin.Context) {
134	        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(c.Request.Context(), d)
135	        defer cancel()
136	        c.Request = c.Request.WithContext(ctx)
137	        c.Next()
138	    }
139	}
140	```
141	
142	Application in `cmd/api/main.go`, applied per route group:
143	
144	| Route group           | Deadline | Rationale                                      |
145	|-----------------------|----------|------------------------------------------------|
146	| `/healthz`, `/readyz` | 1s       | Should be near-instant or treated as down      |
147	| `/api/v1/chat/*`      | 30s      | Streaming chat may hold the request open       |
148	| `/api/v1/upload/*`    | 60s      | Large file uploads                             |
149	| `/api/v1/voice/*`     | 30s      | Voice synth/transcribe over LiveKit            |
150	| Default `/api/v1/*`   | 10s      | Most CRUD-style endpoints                      |
151	
152	### 4. Asynq handlers
153	
154	Each handler entry point wraps its work with `context.WithTimeout` keyed by task type. Where Asynq's native `Timeout` registration suffices we use that instead of inline `WithTimeout`. The exact handler-package path is surveyed during implementation planning (the package containing `asynq.HandlerFunc` registrations); the convention is documented in a short comment at the top of that file.
155	
156	## Error handling
157	
158	- `ErrCircuitOpen` → HTTP 503 + `Retry-After: <BreakerCooldown seconds>`.
159	- `context.DeadlineExceeded` → HTTP 504 (existing behaviour preserved).
160	- Breaker state transitions logged at WARN with structured fields: `service`, `state`, `consecutive_failures`, `policy_name`.
161	- OTel:
162	  - Gauge metric `resilience.breaker.state` (`0=closed, 1=half_open, 2=open`) labeled by `policy_name`.
163	  - Span event `resilience.breaker.transition` on every transition.
164	
165	## Testing strategy
166	
167	### Unit tests
168	
169	- `internal/resilience/policy_test.go` — table-driven:
170	  - Timeout fires when work exceeds `Timeout`.
171	  - Breaker opens after `BreakerThreshold` consecutive failures.
172	  - Breaker stays open for `BreakerCooldown`.
173	  - Breaker transitions to half-open after cooldown; success closes it; failure re-opens it.
174	  - Failures classified correctly (caller errors do not count).
175	- `internal/resilience/grpc_test.go` — interceptor wraps + propagates ctx + applies CB; uses a fake `grpc.UnaryInvoker`.
176	- `internal/middleware/deadline_test.go` — middleware injects deadline; downstream handler observes correct deadline; cancellation propagates.
177	
178	### Integration tests
179	
180	Extend `internal/integration/grpc_fault_test.go` with three new cases:
181	
182	1. **Slow worker** — fault server sleeps 10s; client policy `Timeout=2s`; assert call returns `context.DeadlineExceeded` within ~2s (±100ms tolerance).
183	2. **Breaker opens** — fault server returns `codes.Unavailable` 5×; assert 6th call returns `ErrCircuitOpen` *without* attempting RPC (verify via server-side counter).
184	3. **Half-open recovery** — after breaker opens, sleep `BreakerCooldown`, configure server to return OK; single probe call closes the breaker; subsequent calls go through normally.
185	
186	All tests run under standard `go test ./...` — no docker-compose or external infra required.
187	
188	## CI compliance gate
189	
190	`.golangci.yml` adds two linters at error severity:
191	
192	- `noctx` — flags `http.Get`/`http.Post` and similar without context.
193	- `contextcheck` — flags functions that should accept `ctx context.Context` but don't, and functions that drop a parent context.
194	
195	Scoped to `internal/grpc/`, `internal/handler/`, `internal/service/` via `issues.exclude-rules`. CI fails on any violation.
196	
197	**Optional follow-up (not blocking this milestone):** custom `analysis.Analyzer` plugin in `tools/analyzers/extcall/` that flags `grpc.Dial`, `grpc.NewClient`, `http.NewRequest`, and `http.NewRequestWithContext` outside `internal/resilience/`. Tracked as a follow-up issue.
198	
199	## Compliance / OSPS-L2 alignment
200	
201	- Documents threat: unbounded external calls → resource exhaustion → DoS pathway. Closing this gap is consistent with the OSPS-L2 trajectory already in flight (`docs/osps-l2-compliance-spec`, recent PRs #348, #391).
202	- Adds CI gate that prevents regression — auditable, version-controlled, enforced.
203	- Logged breaker transitions are auditable via OpenObserve.
204	
205	## Configuration knobs (env, with defaults inline)
206	
207	| Variable                              | Default | Used by                                |
208	|---------------------------------------|---------|----------------------------------------|
209	| `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_TIMEOUT`             | `5s`    | gRPC interceptor policy                |
210	| `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_BREAKER_THRESHOLD`   | `5`     | gRPC interceptor policy                |
211	| `RAVEN_AI_WORKER_BREAKER_COOLDOWN`    | `30s`   | gRPC interceptor policy                |
212	| `RAVEN_HTTP_READ_HEADER_TIMEOUT`      | `5s`    | `http.Server.ReadHeaderTimeout`        |
213	| `RAVEN_HTTP_READ_TIMEOUT`             | `30s`   | `http.Server.ReadTimeout`              |
214	| `RAVEN_HTTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT`            | `60s`   | `http.Server.WriteTimeout`             |
215	| `RAVEN_HTTP_IDLE_TIMEOUT`             | `120s`  | `http.Server.IdleTimeout`              |
216	
217	`.env.example` updated with the new variables.
218	
219	## Files added
220	
221	- `internal/resilience/policy.go`
222	- `internal/resilience/policy_test.go`
223	- `internal/resilience/breaker.go`
224	- `internal/resilience/grpc.go`
225	- `internal/resilience/grpc_test.go`
226	- `internal/resilience/http.go`
227	- `internal/middleware/deadline.go`
228	- `internal/middleware/deadline_test.go`
229	
230	## Files modified
231	
232	- `internal/grpc/client.go` — `NewClient` accepts `*resilience.Policy`, wires interceptor.
233	- `internal/grpc/client_test.go` — adapt to new signature.
234	- `internal/integration/grpc_fault_test.go` — add three new test cases.
235	- `cmd/api/main.go` — `http.Server` timeouts; `Deadline` middleware applied per route group.
236	- `internal/apierror/` — map `ErrCircuitOpen` → 503 + `Retry-After` in the file that defines `ErrorHandler` (resolved during plan survey).
237	- The config package (path resolved during plan survey, e.g., `internal/config/`) — surface the new env knobs.
238	- `.env.example` — document new variables.
239	- `.golangci.yml` — enable `noctx` + `contextcheck`.
240	- `go.mod` / `go.sum` — add `github.com/sony/gobreaker`.
241	
242	## Rollout
243	
244	1. Implement on `feat/resilience-layer` worktree branched from `origin/main`.
245	2. Run full local test suite (unit + integration) and `golangci-lint run` before pushing.
246	3. Open PR; CI must be green, including the new linter checks.
247	4. Squash-merge per repo policy. No AI attribution in commit message.
248	5. Auto-merge enqueued at PR creation per `CLAUDE.md`.
249	
250	## Open questions
251	
252	None. All decisions made during brainstorming.
253

1	# Resilience Layer Implementation Plan
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Add bounded-deadline + circuit-breaker resilience to the Raven Go API across the AI-worker gRPC client, `http.Server`, per-route Gin handlers, and Asynq job handlers — with unit + integration tests and a CI compliance gate.
6	
7	**Architecture:** New `internal/resilience/` package owns reusable primitives (`Policy`, breaker adapter, gRPC interceptor, HTTP RoundTripper). New `internal/middleware/deadline.go` enforces per-route context deadlines. `cmd/api/main.go` wires explicit `http.Server` timeouts and the Deadline middleware. `.golangci.yml` adds `noctx` + `contextcheck` as blocking lint gates.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** Go 1.22+, Gin, `google.golang.org/grpc`, `github.com/sony/gobreaker`, `github.com/hibiken/asynq`, `golangci-lint v2`.
10	
11	**Spec:** [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-08-resilience-design.md`](../specs/2026-05-08-resilience-design.md)
12	
13	**Branch:** `feat/resilience-layer` (worktree from `origin/main`).
14

26	| 5 — Asynq + integration | 12, 13 | **yes (2 agents)** | 12 owns jobs/, 13 owns integration/ |

129	- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**
130	
131	Create `internal/resilience/policy_test.go`:
132	
133	```go
134	package resilience
135	
136	import (
137		"errors"
138		"testing"
139		"time"
140	)
141	
142	func TestNewPolicy_Defaults(t *testing.T) {
143		p, err := NewPolicy("ai-worker")
144		if err != nil {
145			t.Fatalf("NewPolicy returned error: %v", err)
146		}
147		if p.Name != "ai-worker" {
148			t.Errorf("Name = %q, want %q", p.Name, "ai-worker")
149		}
150		if p.Timeout != 5*time.Second {

225	- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `policy.go`**
226	
227	Create `internal/resilience/policy.go`:
228	
229	```go
230	// Package resilience provides timeout + circuit-breaker primitives for
231	// bounding external calls (gRPC, HTTP) made by the Raven API.
232	package resilience
233	
234	import (
235		"errors"
236		"fmt"
237		"time"
238	)
239	
240	// ErrInvalidPolicy is returned by NewPolicy when configuration validation fails.
241	var ErrInvalidPolicy = errors.New("resilience: invalid policy")
242	
243	// Policy bundles the timeout and circuit-breaker configuration that gets
244	// applied to a single external dependency (e.g. the AI worker gRPC client).
245	type Policy struct {
246		Name               string
247		Timeout            time.Duration
248		BreakerThreshold   uint32
249		BreakerCooldown    time.Duration
250		BreakerHalfOpenMax uint32
251	}
252	
253	// Option mutates a Policy during construction.
254	type Option func(*Policy)
255	
256	// WithTimeout sets the per-call deadline.
257	func WithTimeout(d time.Duration) Option {
258		return func(p *Policy) { p.Timeout = d }
259	}
260	
261	// WithBreakerThreshold sets the consecutive-failure count that flips the
262	// breaker from Closed to Open.
263	func WithBreakerThreshold(n uint32) Option {
264		return func(p *Policy) { p.BreakerThreshold = n }
265	}
266	
267	// WithBreakerCooldown sets how long the breaker stays Open before the
268	// next probe transitions it to Half-Open.
269	func WithBreakerCooldown(d time.Duration) Option {
270		return func(p *Policy) { p.BreakerCooldown = d }
271	}
272	
273	// WithBreakerHalfOpenMax caps in-flight probes during Half-Open.
274	func WithBreakerHalfOpenMax(n uint32) Option {
275		return func(p *Policy) { p.BreakerHalfOpenMax = n }
276	}
277	
278	// NewPolicy returns a validated Policy. Defaults: 5s timeout,
279	// breaker opens after 5 consecutive failures, 30s cooldown, 1 half-open probe.
280	func NewPolicy(name string, opts ...Option) (*Policy, error) {
281		p := &Policy{
282			Name:               name,
283			Timeout:            5 * time.Second,
284			BreakerThreshold:   5,
285			BreakerCooldown:    30 * time.Second,
286			BreakerHalfOpenMax: 1,
287		}
288		for _, opt := range opts {
289			opt(p)
290		}
291		if err := p.validate(); err != nil {
292			return nil, err
293		}
294		return p, nil
295	}
296	
297	func (p *Policy) validate() error {
298		if p.Name == "" {
299			return fmt.Errorf("%w: name must not be empty", ErrInvalidPolicy)
300		}
301		if p.Timeout <= 0 {
302			return fmt.Errorf("%w: timeout must be > 0", ErrInvalidPolicy)
303		}
304		if p.BreakerThreshold == 0 {
305			return fmt.Errorf("%w: breaker threshold must be > 0", ErrInvalidPolicy)
306		}
307		if p.BreakerCooldown <= 0 {
308			return fmt.Errorf("%w: breaker cooldown must be > 0", ErrInvalidPolicy)
309		}
310		if p.BreakerHalfOpenMax == 0 {
311			return fmt.Errorf("%w: breaker half-open max must be > 0", ErrInvalidPolicy)
312		}
313		return nil
314	}
315	```
316

386		succ := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return "ok", nil }

391		}
392		if _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {
393			t.Fatalf("expected ErrCircuitOpen while open, got %v", err)
394		}
395	
396		// Wait for cooldown.
397		time.Sleep(30 * time.Millisecond)
398	
399		// Half-open probe succeeds → breaker closes.
400		if _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); err != nil {
401			t.Fatalf("half-open probe err = %v, want nil", err)
402		}
403	
404		// Subsequent call should pass.
405		if _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); err != nil {
406			t.Fatalf("post-recovery err = %v, want nil", err)

440	
441	Create `internal/resilience/breaker.go`:
442	
443	```go
444	package resilience
445	
446	import (
447		"context"
448		"errors"
449	
450		"github.com/sony/gobreaker/v2"
451	)
452	
453	// ErrCircuitOpen is returned by a Breaker when the underlying state machine
454	// is Open (or in Half-Open with the probe quota exhausted). Callers should
455	// surface this as HTTP 503 with Retry-After.
456	var ErrCircuitOpen = errors.New("resilience: circuit breaker open")
457	
458	// Breaker is a thin adapter over sony/gobreaker that maps its sentinel
459	// errors to ErrCircuitOpen and respects context cancellation up front.
460	type Breaker struct {
461		cb *gobreaker.CircuitBreaker[any]
462	}
463	
464	// NewBreaker constructs a Breaker from a validated Policy.
465	func NewBreaker(p *Policy) *Breaker {
466		settings := gobreaker.Settings{
467			Name:        p.Name,
468			MaxRequests: p.BreakerHalfOpenMax,
469			Interval:    0, // 0 = never reset counts in Closed state
470			Timeout:     p.BreakerCooldown,
471			ReadyToTrip: func(counts gobreaker.Counts) bool {
472				return counts.ConsecutiveFailures >= p.BreakerThreshold
473			},
474		}
475		return &Breaker{cb: gobreaker.NewCircuitBreaker[any](settings)}
476	}
477	
478	// Execute runs fn through the breaker. It checks ctx cancellation first
479	// to avoid charging the breaker for caller-side cancellations.
480	func (b *Breaker) Execute(ctx context.Context, fn func(context.Context) (any, error)) (any, error) {
481		if err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {
482			return nil, err
483		}
484		out, err := b.cb.Execute(func() (any, error) { return fn(ctx) })
485		switch {
486		case errors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrOpenState),
487			errors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrTooManyRequests):
488			return nil, ErrCircuitOpen
489		}
490		return out, err
491	}
492	```
493

522	package resilience
523	
524	import (
525		"context"
526		"errors"
527		"testing"
528		"time"
529	
530		"google.golang.org/grpc"
531		"google.golang.org/grpc/codes"
532		"google.golang.org/grpc/status"
533	)
534	
535	type fakeInvoker struct {
536		calls int
537		err   error
538		delay time.Duration
539	}
540	
541	func (f *fakeInvoker) invoke(ctx context.Context, _ string, _, _ any, _ *grpc.ClientConn, _ ...grpc.CallOption) error {
542		f.calls++
543		if f.delay > 0 {
544			select {
545			case <-time.After(f.delay):
546			case <-ctx.Done():
547				return ctx.Err()
548			}
549		}
550		return f.err
551	}
552	
553	func TestUnaryClientInterceptor_AppliesTimeout(t *testing.T) {
554		p, _ := NewPolicy("svc", WithTimeout(20*time.Millisecond))
555		icpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, NewBreaker(p))
556	
557		inv := &fakeInvoker{delay: 100 * time.Millisecond}
558		err := icpt(context.Background(), "/svc/Method", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)
559	
560		if !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {
561			t.Errorf("err = %v, want context.DeadlineExceeded", err)
562		}
563	}
564	
565	func TestUnaryClientInterceptor_OpensBreakerOnUnavailable(t *testing.T) {
566		p, _ := NewPolicy("svc",
567			WithTimeout(1*time.Second),
568			WithBreakerThreshold(2),
569			WithBreakerCooldown(100*time.Millisecond),
570		)
571		br := NewBreaker(p)
572		icpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)
573	
574		inv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.Unavailable, "down")}
575	
576		// Two UNAVAILABLE failures should open the breaker.
577		for i := 0; i < 2; i++ {
578			_ = icpt(context.Background(), "/svc/Method", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)
579		}
580		// Third call short-circuits without invoking.
581		preCalls := inv.calls
582		err := icpt(context.Background(), "/svc/Method", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)
583		if !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {
584			t.Errorf("err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen", err)
585		}
586		if inv.calls != preCalls {
587			t.Errorf("invoker called %d times after open; want 0", inv.calls-preCalls)
588		}
589	}
590	
591	func TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsDoNotTrip(t *testing.T) {
592		p, _ := NewPolicy("svc", WithBreakerThreshold(2))
593		br := NewBreaker(p)
594		icpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)
595	
596		inv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, "bad")}
597	
598		// Five caller errors must NOT open the breaker.
599		for i := 0; i < 5; i++ {
600			_ = icpt(context.Background(), "/svc/Method", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)
601		}
602	
603		// A subsequent call should still be invoked (not short-circuited).
604		preCalls := inv.calls
605		_ = icpt(context.Background(), "/svc/Method", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)
606		if inv.calls == preCalls {
607			t.Errorf("breaker tripped on caller errors")
608		}
609	}
610	```

620	- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `grpc.go`**
621	
622	Create `internal/resilience/grpc.go`:
623	
624	```go
625	package resilience
626	
627	import (
628		"context"
629		"time"
630	
631		"google.golang.org/grpc"
632		"google.golang.org/grpc/codes"
633		"google.golang.org/grpc/status"
634	)
635	
636	// UnaryClientInterceptor returns a gRPC unary client interceptor that:
637	//
638	//   - Applies policy.Timeout to each call (only if no shorter deadline is set).
639	//   - Routes the call through the breaker.
640	//   - Counts only server-side failures (Unavailable, DeadlineExceeded,
641	//     Internal, ResourceExhausted) toward the breaker's failure tally;
642	//     caller errors (InvalidArgument, NotFound, PermissionDenied,
643	//     Unauthenticated) are not counted.
644	func UnaryClientInterceptor(p *Policy, br *Breaker) grpc.UnaryClientInterceptor {
645		return func(
646			ctx context.Context,
647			method string,
648			req, reply any,
649			cc *grpc.ClientConn,
650			invoker grpc.UnaryInvoker,
651			opts ...grpc.CallOption,
652		) error {
653			// Apply policy timeout unless a shorter deadline already exists.
654			callCtx, cancel := withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx, p.Timeout)
655			defer cancel()
656	
657			_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {
658				invErr := invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)
659				if isCallerError(invErr) {
660					// Tell gobreaker the call succeeded so caller errors don't trip it.
661					return nil, nil
662				}
663				return nil, invErr
664			})
665			return err
666		}
667	}
668	
669	func withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {
670		if dl, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && time.Until(dl) <= d {
671			return context.WithCancel(ctx)
672		}
673		return context.WithTimeout(ctx, d)
674	}
675	
676	func isCallerError(err error) bool {
677		if err == nil {
678			return false
679		}
680		switch status.Code(err) {
681		case codes.InvalidArgument,
682			codes.NotFound,
683			codes.AlreadyExists,
684			codes.PermissionDenied,
685			codes.Unauthenticated,
686			codes.FailedPrecondition,
687			codes.OutOfRange:
688			return true
689		}
690		return false
691	}
692	```
693

783	- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `http.go`**
784	
785	Create `internal/resilience/http.go`:
786	
787	```go
788	package resilience
789	
790	import (
791		"errors"
792		"fmt"
793		"net"
794		"net/http"
795		"time"
796	)
797	
798	// HTTPClient returns an *http.Client configured with the policy timeout
799	// and a transport with sensible per-stage timeouts. Use this for any
800	// outbound HTTP call (LiveKit, SeaweedFS, third-party APIs).
801	func HTTPClient(p *Policy) *http.Client {
802		return &http.Client{
803			Timeout:   p.Timeout,
804			Transport: defaultTransport(),
805		}
806	}
807	
808	// HTTPClientWithBreaker wraps HTTPClient's transport with a breaker-aware
809	// RoundTripper. 5xx responses count toward breaker failures.
810	func HTTPClientWithBreaker(p *Policy, br *Breaker) *http.Client {
811		return &http.Client{
812			Timeout: p.Timeout,
813			Transport: &breakerTransport{
814				next:    defaultTransport(),
815				breaker: br,
816			},
817		}
818	}
819	
820	func defaultTransport() *http.Transport {
821		return &http.Transport{
822			DialContext: (&net.Dialer{
823				Timeout:   3 * time.Second,
824				KeepAlive: 30 * time.Second,
825			}).DialContext,
826			TLSHandshakeTimeout:   3 * time.Second,
827			ResponseHeaderTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
828			ExpectContinueTimeout: 1 * time.Second,
829			IdleConnTimeout:       90 * time.Second,
830			MaxIdleConns:          100,
831			MaxIdleConnsPerHost:   10,
832		}
833	}
834	
835	type breakerTransport struct {
836		next    http.RoundTripper
837		breaker *Breaker
838	}
839	
840	func (t *breakerTransport) RoundTrip(req *http.Request) (*http.Response, error) {
841		out, err := t.breaker.Execute(req.Context(), func(_ context.Context) (any, error) {
842			resp, rerr := t.next.RoundTrip(req)
843			if rerr != nil {
844				return nil, rerr
845			}
846			if resp.StatusCode >= 500 {
847				return resp, fmt.Errorf("upstream %d", resp.StatusCode)
848			}
849			return resp, nil
850		})
851		if errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {
852			return nil, err
853		}
854		if err != nil {
855			// breakerTransport.Execute may return a non-nil response alongside
856			// the synthetic 5xx error; surface that response.
857			if r, ok := out.(*http.Response); ok && r != nil {
858				return r, nil
859			}
860			return nil, err
861		}
862		resp, _ := out.(*http.Response)
863		return resp, nil
864	}
865	```
866	
867	Add `"context"` to the import block alongside `errors`, `fmt`, `net`, `net/http`, `time`.
868

873	```

966	
967	Create `internal/middleware/deadline.go`:
968	
969	```go
970	package middleware
971	
972	import (
973		"context"
974		"time"
975	
976		"github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
977	)
978	
979	// Deadline returns a Gin middleware that wraps the request context
980	// in context.WithTimeout(d). Apply at the route group level so each
981	// group can have its own budget.
982	func Deadline(d time.Duration) gin.HandlerFunc {
983		return func(c *gin.Context) {
984			ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(c.Request.Context(), d)
985			defer cancel()
986			c.Request = c.Request.WithContext(ctx)
987			c.Next()
988		}
989	}
990	```

1205	Replace `internal/grpc/client.go` with:
1206	
1207	```go
1208	// Package grpc provides a gRPC client for communicating with the Python AI worker.
1209	package grpc
1210	
1211	import (
1212		"google.golang.org/grpc"
1213		"google.golang.org/grpc/credentials/insecure"
1214	
1215		pb "github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/grpc/pb"
1216		"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/resilience"
1217	)
1218	
1219	// Client wraps a gRPC connection and exposes the AIWorker service stub.
1220	type Client struct {
1221		conn   *grpc.ClientConn
1222		worker pb.AIWorkerClient
1223	}
1224	
1225	// NewClient dials the AI worker at addr and returns a ready-to-use Client.
1226	// The unary interceptor wires policy.Timeout and the breaker around every call.
1227	func NewClient(addr string, policy *resilience.Policy, breaker *resilience.Breaker) (*Client, error) {
1228		conn, err := grpc.NewClient(
1229			addr,
1230			grpc.WithTransportCredentials(insecure.NewCredentials()),
1231			grpc.WithChainUnaryInterceptor(resilience.UnaryClientInterceptor(policy, breaker)),
1232		)
1233		if err != nil {
1234			return nil, err
1235		}
1236		return &Client{conn: conn, worker: pb.NewAIWorkerClient(conn)}, nil
1237	}
1238	
1239	// Worker returns the AIWorkerClient stub for making RPC calls.
1240	func (c *Client) Worker() pb.AIWorkerClient { return c.worker }
1241	
1242	// Close releases the underlying gRPC connection.
1243	func (c *Client) Close() error { return c.conn.Close() }
1244	```
1245	
1246	(Confirm the module path `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven` matches `go.mod` — adjust if different.)

1390	
1391	Sized per spec:
1392	
1393	| Route group           | Budget                                |
1394	|-----------------------|---------------------------------------|
1395	| `/healthz`, `/readyz` | `1 * time.Second`                     |
1396	| `/api/v1/chat/*`      | `30 * time.Second`                    |
1397	| `/api/v1/upload/*`    | `60 * time.Second`                    |
1398	| `/api/v1/voice/*`     | `30 * time.Second`                    |
1399	| Default `/api/v1/*`   | `10 * time.Second`                    |
1400	
1401	For sub-groups that need a different budget than their parent, call `chatAPI.Use(middleware.Deadline(30 * time.Second))` on the sub-group — Gin's `Use` is additive but the inner Deadline will override the outer one because `context.WithTimeout` only shortens.

1523	func TestResilience_SlowAIWorker_HitsClientDeadline(t *testing.T) {
1524		srv, addr := startFaultServer(t, faultConfig{Delay: 5 * time.Second})

1529		)

1534		}
1535		defer client.Close()
1536	
1537		start := time.Now()
1538		_, err = client.Worker().SomeMethod(context.Background(), &pb.SomeRequest{})
1539		elapsed := time.Since(start)
1540

1542			t.Errorf("err code = %v, want DeadlineExceeded", status.Code(err))
1543		}
1544		if elapsed > 400*time.Millisecond {
1545			t.Errorf("call took %v; expected ≤ ~200ms", elapsed)
1546		}
1547	}
1548	
1549	func TestResilience_RepeatedUnavailable_OpensBreaker(t *testing.T) {
1550		srv, addr := startFaultServer(t, faultConfig{Code: codes.Unavailable})
1551		defer srv.Stop()
1552	
1553		policy, _ := resilience.NewPolicy("ai-worker",
1554			resilience.WithTimeout(500*time.Millisecond),
1555			resilience.WithBreakerThreshold(3),
1556			resilience.WithBreakerCooldown(2*time.Second),
1557		)
1558		breaker := resilience.NewBreaker(policy)
1559		client, err := rpcClient.NewClient(addr, policy, breaker)
1560		if err != nil {
1561			t.Fatal(err)
1562		}
1563		defer client.Close()
1564	
1565		for i := 0; i < 3; i++ {
1566			_, _ = client.Worker().SomeMethod(context.Background(), &pb.SomeRequest{})
1567		}
1568	
1569		preCalls := srv.CallCount()
1570		_, err = client.Worker().SomeMethod(context.Background(), &pb.SomeRequest{})
1571		if !errors.Is(err, resilience.ErrCircuitOpen) {
1572			t.Errorf("err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen", err)
1573		}
1574		if srv.CallCount() != preCalls {
1575			t.Errorf("server saw extra call after breaker opened")
1576		}
1577	}
1578	
1579	func TestResilience_HalfOpenProbe_ClosesBreaker(t *testing.T) {
1580		srv, addr := startFaultServer(t, faultConfig{Code: codes.Unavailable})
1581		defer srv.Stop()
1582	
1583		policy, _ := resilience.NewPolicy("ai-worker",
1584			resilience.WithTimeout(500*time.Millisecond),
1585			resilience.WithBreakerThreshold(2),
1586			resilience.WithBreakerCooldown(100*time.Millisecond),
1587			resilience.WithBreakerHalfOpenMax(1),

1589		breaker := resilience.NewBreaker(policy)

1596		// Trip the breaker.

1598			_, _ = client.Worker().SomeMethod(context.Background(), &pb.SomeRequest{})
1599		}
1600	
1601		// Wait for cooldown, then flip server to OK.
1602		time.Sleep(150 * time.Millisecond)
1603		srv.SetConfig(faultConfig{Code: codes.OK})
1604	
1605		// Probe call should succeed and close the breaker.

1612			t.Errorf("post-recovery err = %v", err)
1613		}
1614	}

1	// Package grpc provides a gRPC client for communicating with the Python AI worker.
2	package grpc
3	
4	import (
5		"google.golang.org/grpc"
6		"google.golang.org/grpc/credentials/insecure"
7	
8		pb "github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/grpc/pb"
9	)
10	
11	// Client wraps a gRPC connection and exposes the AIWorker service stub.
12	type Client struct {
13		conn   *grpc.ClientConn
14		worker pb.AIWorkerClient
15	}
16	
17	// NewClient dials the AI worker at addr and returns a ready-to-use Client.
18	func NewClient(addr string) (*Client, error) {
19		conn, err := grpc.NewClient(addr, grpc.WithTransportCredentials(insecure.NewCredentials()))
20		if err != nil {
21			return nil, err
22		}
23		return &Client{conn: conn, worker: pb.NewAIWorkerClient(conn)}, nil
24	}
25	
26	// Worker returns the AIWorkerClient stub for making RPC calls.
27	func (c *Client) Worker() pb.AIWorkerClient { return c.worker }
28	
29	// Close releases the underlying gRPC connection.
30	func (c *Client) Close() error { return c.conn.Close() }
31	
```

I've cross-referenced the spec + plan against the actual code. Several real issues to resolve. Starting with the most foundational, walking down the tree one at a time.

---

### Question 1: Where does the HTTP error mapping for `ErrCircuitOpen` actually live?

**The problem.** The spec says:
> `apierror.ErrorHandler` maps `ErrCircuitOpen` to HTTP 503 with `Retry-After`

And Task 10 says "Modify the file in the apierror package that defines `ErrorHandler`."

**But:** there is no `internal/apierror/` package in the repo, and no `func ErrorHandler` exists anywhere:

```
$ find internal -type d -name apierror
(empty)
$ grep -rn 'func ErrorHandler' --include='*.go' internal/ cmd/
(empty)
```

So Task 10 has no target. The breaker primitive would land but `ErrCircuitOpen` would propagate through Gin as a generic 500 (or get logged + dropped depending on what `c.Error()` callers actually do here).

**Why this matters.** The whole "503 + `Retry-After` so callers back off" story — one of the spec's stated goals — has no implementation home until this is resolved. Whatever we decide cascades into the test in Task 10 step 4 and the contract callers see.

**Recommended answer: pick (B).** Most repos this size already have an error-mapping layer somewhere even if it's not called `ErrorHandler` — usually a custom Gin recovery middleware or a per-handler `if err != nil { c.JSON(...) }` pattern. Spend 5 minutes finding where the AI worker's `codes.Unavailable` *currently* surfaces to the client, and put the `ErrCircuitOpen` mapping next to it. Creating a brand-new `apierror/` package just for this introduces a second error-mapping convention nobody else uses.

> AGENT

Survey result — and a wrinkle. The `apierror` package **does** exist, just at `pkg/apierror/`, not `internal/apierror/`. And there's already an `ErrorHandler` middleware at `pkg/apierror/apierror.go:100`. **The spec has the wrong path.**

But the existing `ErrorHandler` is type-assertion based, not `errors.Is`-based:
```go
if quotaErr, ok := err.(*QuotaError); ok { c.JSON(quotaErr.Code, quotaErr) }
else if appErr, ok := err.(*AppError); ok { c.JSON(appErr.Code, appErr) }
else { /* 500 fallback */ }
```

So the plan's `case errors.Is(err, resilience.ErrCircuitOpen):` won't fit — it'd need to be inserted *before* the type-assertions, and `*AppError` doesn't have a slot for a `Retry-After` header (it's body-only JSON today). Two distinct ways to land this:

---

### Question 2: How should `ErrCircuitOpen` plug into the existing `ErrorHandler`?

**Recommended answer: (A).** Adding an `errors.Is(err, resilience.ErrCircuitOpen)` branch at the top of `ErrorHandler` is one place to maintain, doesn't ask every handler to translate the sentinel, and matches the spec's intent. The Go `internal` import rule is satisfied (`pkg/apierror` is a sibling of `internal/` under the module root). The only real cost is `pkg/apierror` now imports `internal/resilience` — a one-way coupling that's fine for a single-module repo not consumed externally.

**Trade-off note.** (B) preserves a cleaner pkg/internal layering but pushes the work outward; every gRPC-calling handler has to know the rule. (C) is purist but probably overkill.

---

### Question 3: The gRPC interceptor swallows caller errors entirely

**The bug.** Task 4's `grpc.go` does this:
```go
_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {
    invErr := invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)
    if isCallerError(invErr) {
        return nil, nil  // ← tells gobreaker "success"
    }
    return nil, invErr
})
return err  // ← err is now nil for caller errors
```

When the AI worker returns `codes.InvalidArgument` (or `NotFound`, `PermissionDenied`, etc.), `br.Execute` returns `(nil, nil)` and the interceptor returns `nil` to the caller. **The actual gRPC error never reaches the handler.** Handlers think `client.Worker().Embed(...)` succeeded, then dereference a nil reply struct → panic.

The plan's own test catches this in spirit (`TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsDoNotTrip` only checks the breaker stays closed; it never asserts the error is propagated).

**Recommended answer: (A).** `gobreaker.Settings` has an `IsSuccessful func(err error) bool` hook for exactly this reason — it lets the breaker classify an error as "doesn't count" while still propagating it to the caller. The interceptor stops swallowing; the breaker still ignores caller errors. Add an `IsSuccessful` predicate to the breaker constructor (or expose it via Policy), and have the interceptor return `invErr` unconditionally.

Two follow-on decisions baked in: where does the caller-error classifier live (in `internal/resilience` so the breaker primitive stays generic, with the gRPC code passing the right predicate), and do we add a positive test that asserts the error propagates (we should).

        },

        }

### Question 4: Per-handler Asynq timeouts — wrong shape and incomplete coverage

**The mismatch.** Task 12's grep is `ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, task \*asynq.Task)`. That misses three real handlers:

| File | Shape | Caught by Task 12? |
|---|---|---|
| `voice_usage.go`, `usage.go`, `cleanup.go`, `recrawl.go`, `webhook_delivery.go` | `func (h *X) ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, ...)` | ✅ |
| `airbyte_sync.go` | `func (h *X) ProcessTask(_ context.Context, ...)` (drops ctx) | ❌ (pattern miss + ctx is dropped → deadline ineffective) |
| `document_process.go`, `email_summary.go`, `send_email.go` | `func HandleX(deps) asynq.HandlerFunc` (returns a closure) | ❌ |

So 4 of 9 handlers wouldn't get timeouts. And `airbyte_sync.go` has an independent bug: it ignores the context entirely, so even if Task 12 wrapped it, the deadline wouldn't fire inside the work loop.

**Asynq idiom matters.** Spec ¶ "Asynq handlers" says: *"Where Asynq's native `Timeout` registration suffices we use that instead of inline `WithTimeout`."* — but Task 12 ignores that and goes for inline wrapping anyway. Asynq's idiomatic mechanism is `asynq.Timeout(...)` at *task construction* time (passed to `asynq.NewTask`), and the server enforces it. There's also `mux.Use(...)` middleware, which is a clean global hook.

**Recommended answer: (A).** Add a single Asynq middleware (one place, catches every handler regardless of shape) AND fix `airbyte_sync.go` to actually honour ctx. The middleware can read a per-task-type budget from a small map (`document_process: 5m`, `voice_usage: 30s`, etc.) so we keep per-handler granularity without touching 9 files.

> AGENT

**Surprising finding — the blast radius is zero.** All four target packages (`internal/handler`, `internal/service`, `internal/grpc`, `internal/jobs`) pass `noctx` + `contextcheck` clean today (verified with `golangci-lint run --no-config --default=none -E noctx -E contextcheck`). The plan's defensive language about baselining violations and writing nolint directives was speculative; in practice there's nothing to clean up.

That removes a whole risk class — Task 8 is now a near-trivial config change. So the real remaining design question is scope.

---

### Question 5: Should the lint gate scope match the spec exactly, or widen?

Spec scopes the new linters to `internal/grpc/`, `internal/handler/`, `internal/service/` via `issues.exclude-rules`. Since the blast radius is 0, widening to also cover `cmd/api/`, `internal/middleware/`, `internal/jobs/` (and even `internal/repository/`, `pkg/`) costs nothing now and catches future regressions in places that *also* hold external calls (cmd/api wires HTTP clients; internal/jobs hits gRPC and HTTP from handlers; pkg/apierror is about to start importing internal/resilience).

**Recommended answer: (B) — widen to all production Go.** The linters apply globally with no exclusions; tests are excluded by golangci-lint's defaults. This is the moment to set the standard, and it costs nothing.

### Question 6: The per-route deadline table doesn't match the actual routes

Spec/Task 11 give this deadline budget table:

| Route group           | Deadline |
|-----------------------|----------|
| `/healthz`, `/readyz` | 1s       |
| `/api/v1/chat/*`      | 30s      |
| `/api/v1/upload/*`    | 60s      |
| `/api/v1/voice/*`     | 30s      |
| Default `/api/v1/*`   | 10s      |

What's actually in `cmd/api/main.go`:

| Real path/group | Line | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `router.GET("/healthz", ...)` | 496 | exists; standalone, not in a group |
| `/readyz` | — | **does not exist** |
| `chatAPI := router.Group("/api/v1/chat")` | 794 | matches spec |
| `/api/v1/upload/*` | — | **does not exist as a group**; upload-shaped work lives inside `documents` (line 586) and `sources` (576), nested 4 levels deep under `api/orgs/:org_id/workspaces/:ws_id/knowledge-bases/:kb_id/...` |
| `/api/v1/voice/*` | — | Real path is `/api/v1/orgs/:org_id/voice-sessions` (line 717) — also nested |
| `api := router.Group("/api/v1")` | 517 | catches everything except chat/admin/standalone |
| `admin := router.Group("/api/v1/admin")` | 825 | separate group, not in spec table |
| Standalone POSTs: `/api/v1/billing/webhook`, `/webhooks/meta`, `/api/v1/auth/callback`, `/api/v1/notifications/unsubscribe` | 821, 822, 841, 502 | not in any group; spec doesn't address |

The plan would either fail to find `/readyz`/`/upload/*`/`/voice/*` or apply deadlines at the wrong level (overly generous or overly restrictive). The webhook endpoints — third-party POSTs from Stripe/Razorpay/Meta — also have specific deadline needs (you don't want a slow webhook receiver to hold a connection open).

**Recommended answer: (A) — adapt the table to real routes.** Map spec intent to actual groups, drop `/readyz`, push the upload/voice budgets to the deepest matching nested groups, add explicit deadlines for the standalone webhook endpoints (10s feels right), and apply the default 10s to the outer `api` group. Document the actual mapping inline in the plan.

### Question 7: Task 5's HTTP factory ships with no callers

Task 5 builds `internal/resilience/http.go` (`HTTPClient`, `HTTPClientWithBreaker`, `breakerTransport`) — but the spec explicitly says:

> A wholesale audit of every outbound HTTP client construction site (LiveKit, SeaweedFS) — only the callsites we touch as part of this milestone get refactored to the new factory. Remaining audit moves to a follow-up.

And no task wires the new HTTP client into any of the callsites the plan modifies. Net effect: ~80 lines of code + tests merge as dead code, with `breakerTransport.RoundTrip` having a non-trivial bug-prone branch (the success-with-5xx-response case where the wrapped fn returns `(resp, syntheticErr)` and the code type-asserts back out).

Three options. None are clearly wrong; depends on how much you trust YAGNI.

**Recommended answer: (A) — drop Task 5 from this milestone.** Ship gRPC interceptor + breaker primitive only. When the HTTP audit follow-up actually has a caller (LiveKit, SeaweedFS, Razorpay), build the HTTP variant *then* with the real call shape in mind. Cuts ~150 LOC, removes the speculative `breakerTransport` corner case, and keeps the milestone tightly scoped.

### Question 8: OTel observability is in the spec but no task implements it

Spec ¶ "Error handling" requires:
> - Gauge metric `resilience.breaker.state` (`0=closed, 1=half_open, 2=open`) labeled by `policy_name`.
> - Span event `resilience.breaker.transition` on every transition.

Tasks 2–15 don't implement either. `gobreaker` exposes a `OnStateChange func(name string, from State, to State)` callback that's the natural hook for both — so the work is small but real (need an OTel meter for the gauge, a tracer for the span event, plumb both into `NewBreaker`).

Per memory, observability stack is **OpenObserve** (already wired in this codebase via OTel). So the integration target exists.

**Recommended answer: (A) — ship it inline.** It's small (~30 LOC + 1 test), the spec listed it as part of "Error handling" not "Non-Goals", and operationally it's what makes a circuit breaker auditable in OpenObserve dashboards (which is what the spec's "Compliance / OSPS-L2 alignment" section relies on). Adding it later means a follow-up PR with all the same test scaffolding. Just wire it now.

## Grilling done. Summary of resolutions

Eight design branches resolved. Net effect on the plan:

| # | Topic | Decision | Plan change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Error mapping package | Use existing `pkg/apierror` (not new `internal/apierror/`) | Task 10 retargeted |
| 2 | `ErrCircuitOpen` wiring | Add `errors.Is` branch at top of existing `ErrorHandler` | Task 10 rewritten; `pkg/apierror` imports `internal/resilience` |
| 3 | Caller-error swallowing bug | Use `gobreaker.Settings.IsSuccessful` predicate | Task 4 grpc.go rewritten; new test asserts error propagates |
| 4 | Asynq coverage | `mux.Use` middleware + per-task-type budget map; fix `airbyte_sync` ctx-drop | Task 12 rewritten — single edit point instead of 9 |
| 5 | Lint scope | Widen `noctx` + `contextcheck` to all production Go (blast radius verified = 0) | Task 8 simplified — no `exclude-rules` needed |
| 6 | Route deadlines | Adapt to real routes (`/healthz` standalone, `chatAPI` line 794, drop `/readyz`, push upload/voice budgets into nested groups, explicit deadlines for standalone webhooks) | Task 11 step 2 rewritten with line-anchored table |
| 7 | HTTP factory | Drop Task 5 entirely — defer to follow-up audit when there's a real caller | Removes ~150 LOC of dead code; renumber tasks |
| 8 | OTel observability | Ship inline — `gobreaker.OnStateChange` → meter + tracer | New small task between current 3 and 4 |

**ADR candidates** (per the skill's hard-to-reverse + surprising + real-trade-off rule):

- **ADR-A**: "Resilience errors map to HTTP via `pkg/apierror.ErrorHandler` branch" — surprising (pkg importing internal/resilience) and a real trade-off (Q2 had 4 options).
- **ADR-B**: "Asynq per-task deadlines via `mux.Use` middleware, not `asynq.Timeout` at NewTask sites" — surprising (the Asynq-idiomatic answer would be `asynq.Timeout`) and a real trade-off (Q4 had 4 options).

The other six decisions are either reversible polish or unsurprising (the breaker primitive choice, the lint scope, the route mapping).

**No `CONTEXT.md` updates needed** — none of the resolved terms are durable domain language. They're all implementation patterns ("circuit breaker", "resilience policy", "caller error") that belong in code comments, not the glossary.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-05-08-resilience-layer.md",
  "old_string": "## Task 3: `resilience.Breaker` adapter (parallel-safe)\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `internal/resilience/breaker.go`\n- Test: `internal/resilience/breaker_test.go`\n\nOwns only files in `internal/resilience/breaker*.go` — disjoint from Tasks 2, 6, 7, 8.",
  "new_string": "## Task 3: `resilience.Breaker` adapter + OTel observability (parallel-safe)\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `internal/resilience/breaker.go`\n- Test: `internal/resilience/breaker_test.go`\n\nOwns only files in `internal/resilience/breaker*.go` — disjoint from Tasks 2, 6, 7, 8.\n\n**Two adjustments from the original spec, both surfaced in grilling:**\n\n1. The breaker accepts an `IsSuccessful` predicate via `WithIsSuccessful(...)`. This is the hook gobreaker already provides for \"this error doesn't count toward the breaker.\" Task 4's gRPC interceptor passes a predicate that classifies caller-side gRPC codes (`InvalidArgument`, `NotFound`, etc.) as success-equivalent, so those errors propagate to the caller AND don't trip the breaker. Without this hook, the original Task 4 design swallowed caller errors entirely (correctness bug).\n2. The breaker wires `gobreaker.OnStateChange` to OTel — gauge `resilience.breaker.state` (0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open) and span event `resilience.breaker.transition`. The spec listed these under \"Error handling\" but no original task implemented them. Folded in here because OnStateChange is set inside `gobreaker.Settings`, which is built inside `NewBreaker` — there's no clean separate file to put it in."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `breaker.go`**\n\nCreate `internal/resilience/breaker.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sony/gobreaker/v2\"\n)\n\n// ErrCircuitOpen is returned by a Breaker when the underlying state machine\n// is Open (or in Half-Open with the probe quota exhausted). Callers should\n// surface this as HTTP 503 with Retry-After.\nvar ErrCircuitOpen = errors.New(\"resilience: circuit breaker open\")\n\n// Breaker is a thin adapter over sony/gobreaker that maps its sentinel\n// errors to ErrCircuitOpen and respects context cancellation up front.\ntype Breaker struct {\n\tcb *gobreaker.CircuitBreaker[any]\n}\n\n// NewBreaker constructs a Breaker from a validated Policy.\nfunc NewBreaker(p *Policy) *Breaker {\n\tsettings := gobreaker.Settings{\n\t\tName:        p.Name,\n\t\tMaxRequests: p.BreakerHalfOpenMax,\n\t\tInterval:    0, // 0 = never reset counts in Closed state\n\t\tTimeout:     p.BreakerCooldown,\n\t\tReadyToTrip: func(counts gobreaker.Counts) bool {\n\t\t\treturn counts.ConsecutiveFailures >= p.BreakerThreshold\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\treturn &Breaker{cb: gobreaker.NewCircuitBreaker[any](settings)}\n}\n\n// Execute runs fn through the breaker. It checks ctx cancellation first\n// to avoid charging the breaker for caller-side cancellations.\nfunc (b *Breaker) Execute(ctx context.Context, fn func(context.Context) (any, error)) (any, error) {\n\tif err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tout, err := b.cb.Execute(func() (any, error) { return fn(ctx) })\n\tswitch {\n\tcase errors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrOpenState),\n\t\terrors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrTooManyRequests):\n\t\treturn nil, ErrCircuitOpen\n\t}\n\treturn out, err\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `breaker.go`**\n\nCreate `internal/resilience/breaker.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sony/gobreaker/v2\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/attribute\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/metric\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/trace\"\n)\n\n// ErrCircuitOpen is returned by a Breaker when the underlying state machine\n// is Open (or in Half-Open with the probe quota exhausted). Callers should\n// surface this as HTTP 503 with Retry-After.\nvar ErrCircuitOpen = errors.New(\"resilience: circuit breaker open\")\n\n// Breaker is a thin adapter over sony/gobreaker that maps its sentinel\n// errors to ErrCircuitOpen and respects context cancellation up front.\ntype Breaker struct {\n\tcb *gobreaker.CircuitBreaker[any]\n}\n\n// BreakerOption configures NewBreaker. All options are optional; defaults\n// match the upstream gobreaker behaviour with our error classification.\ntype BreakerOption func(*breakerOpts)\n\ntype breakerOpts struct {\n\tisSuccessful func(error) bool\n\tmeter        metric.Meter\n\ttracer       trace.Tracer\n}\n\n// WithIsSuccessful classifies errors that should NOT count as breaker\n// failures (e.g. gRPC InvalidArgument is a caller bug, not a server fault).\n// The error still propagates to the caller; the breaker just ignores it.\nfunc WithIsSuccessful(fn func(error) bool) BreakerOption {\n\treturn func(o *breakerOpts) { o.isSuccessful = fn }\n}\n\n// WithObservability wires gobreaker's OnStateChange callback to OTel.\n// Emits a Gauge `resilience.breaker.state` (0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open)\n// labeled by policy name, plus a span event `resilience.breaker.transition`\n// on every transition. Both meter and tracer must be non-nil; if either\n// is nil this option is a no-op.\nfunc WithObservability(meter metric.Meter, tracer trace.Tracer) BreakerOption {\n\treturn func(o *breakerOpts) {\n\t\tif meter != nil && tracer != nil {\n\t\t\to.meter = meter\n\t\t\to.tracer = tracer\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// NewBreaker constructs a Breaker from a validated Policy.\nfunc NewBreaker(p *Policy, opts ...BreakerOption) *Breaker {\n\to := &breakerOpts{}\n\tfor _, opt := range opts {\n\t\topt(o)\n\t}\n\n\tsettings := gobreaker.Settings{\n\t\tName:        p.Name,\n\t\tMaxRequests: p.BreakerHalfOpenMax,\n\t\tInterval:    0, // 0 = never reset counts in Closed state\n\t\tTimeout:     p.BreakerCooldown,\n\t\tReadyToTrip: func(counts gobreaker.Counts) bool {\n\t\t\treturn counts.ConsecutiveFailures >= p.BreakerThreshold\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\tif o.isSuccessful != nil {\n\t\tsettings.IsSuccessful = o.isSuccessful\n\t}\n\tif o.meter != nil {\n\t\t// Build a current-state holder closed over by both the gauge\n\t\t// observer and the OnStateChange callback.\n\t\tvar currentState gobreaker.State\n\t\tgauge, err := o.meter.Int64ObservableGauge(\"resilience.breaker.state\",\n\t\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Circuit breaker state: 0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open\"))\n\t\tif err == nil {\n\t\t\t_, _ = o.meter.RegisterCallback(func(_ context.Context, obs metric.Observer) error {\n\t\t\t\tobs.ObserveInt64(gauge, int64(currentState),\n\t\t\t\t\tmetric.WithAttributes(attribute.String(\"policy_name\", p.Name)))\n\t\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t\t}, gauge)\n\t\t}\n\t\tsettings.OnStateChange = func(name string, from, to gobreaker.State) {\n\t\t\tcurrentState = to\n\t\t\t_, span := o.tracer.Start(context.Background(), \"resilience.breaker.transition\")\n\t\t\tspan.SetAttributes(\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"policy_name\", name),\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"from\", from.String()),\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"to\", to.String()),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tspan.End()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn &Breaker{cb: gobreaker.NewCircuitBreaker[any](settings)}\n}\n\n// Execute runs fn through the breaker. It checks ctx cancellation first\n// to avoid charging the breaker for caller-side cancellations.\nfunc (b *Breaker) Execute(ctx context.Context, fn func(context.Context) (any, error)) (any, error) {\n\tif err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tout, err := b.cb.Execute(func() (any, error) { return fn(ctx) })\n\tswitch {\n\tcase errors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrOpenState),\n\t\terrors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrTooManyRequests):\n\t\treturn nil, ErrCircuitOpen\n\t}\n\treturn out, err\n}\n```\n\nAdd at least one new test to `breaker_test.go` covering the `WithIsSuccessful` predicate:\n\n```go\nfunc TestBreaker_IsSuccessfulPredicate(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithBreakerThreshold(2))\n\tbr := NewBreaker(p, WithIsSuccessful(func(err error) bool {\n\t\treturn errors.Is(err, errCallerFault)\n\t}))\n\n\tcallerFail := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errCallerFault }\n\n\t// Five caller-classified failures must NOT trip the breaker.\n\tfor i := 0; i < 5; i++ {\n\t\t_, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), callerFail)\n\t\tif !errors.Is(err, errCallerFault) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"err = %v, want errCallerFault to propagate\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Verify the breaker is still closed: a real failure flips it.\n\trealFail := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errors.New(\"server boom\") }\n\tfor i := 0; i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_, _ = br.Execute(context.Background(), realFail)\n\t}\n\tif _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), realFail); !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"breaker did not open after 2 server failures; err = %v\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nvar errCallerFault = errors.New(\"caller fault\")\n```\n\n(OTel observability is exercised end-to-end in Task 13's integration tests via the metric reader; a unit test would mostly assert plumbing.)"
}

i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\t}\n\t// Third call short-circuits without invoking.\n\tpreCalls := inv.calls\n\terr := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen\", err)\n\t}\n\tif inv.calls != preCalls {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"invoker called %d times after open;   "new_string": "- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**\n\nCreate `internal/resilience/grpc_test.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/codes\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/status\"\n)\n\ntype fakeInvoker struct {\n\tcalls int\n\terr   error\n\tdelay time.Duration\n}\n\nfunc (f *fakeInvoker) invoke(ctx context.Context, _ string, _, _ any, _ *grpc.ClientConn, _ ...grpc.CallOption) error {\n\tf.calls++\n\tif f.delay > 0 {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(f.delay):\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn f.err\n}\n\nfunc newGRPCBreaker(p *Policy) *Breaker {\n\treturn NewBreaker(p, WithIsSuccessful(IsGRPCCallerError))\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_AppliesTimeout(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithTimeout(20*time.Millisecond))\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, newGRPCBreaker(p))\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{delay: 100 * time.Millisecond}\n\terr := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\n\tif !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want context.DeadlineExceeded\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_OpensBreakerOnUnavailable(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\",\n\t\tWithTimeout(1*time.Second),\n\t\tWithBreakerThreshold(2),\n\t\tWithBreakerCooldown(100*time.Millisecond),\n\t)\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.Unavailable, \"down\")}\n\n\t// Two UNAVAILABLE failures should open the breaker.\n\tfor i := 0; i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\t}\n\t// Third call short-circuits without invoking.\n\tpreCalls := inv.calls\n\terr := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen\", err)\n\t}\n\tif inv.calls != preCalls {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"invoker called %d times after open; want 0\", inv.calls-preCalls)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsDoNotTrip(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithBreakerThreshold(2))\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, \"bad\")}\n\n\t// Five caller errors must NOT open the breaker.\n\tfor i := 0; i < 5; i++ {\n\t\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\t}\n\n\t// A subsequent call should still be invoked (not short-circuited).\n\tpreCalls := inv.calls\n\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif inv.calls == preCalls {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"breaker tripped on caller errors\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// Regression: caller errors must propagate to the caller verbatim.\n// The original (pre-grilling) interceptor returned nil to the caller for\n// caller-classified errors, which would cause handlers to dereference a\n// nil reply and panic.\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsPropagate(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\")\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\twant := status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, \"bad input\")\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: want}\n\n\tgot := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif got == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"interceptor returned nil; want the caller error to propagate\")\n\t}\n\tif status.Code(got) != codes.InvalidArgument {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"propagated code = %v, want InvalidArgument\", status.Code(got))\n\t}\n}\n```"

  "old_string": "- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `grpc.go`**\n\nCreate `internal/resilience/grpc.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/codes\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/status\"\n)\n\n// UnaryClientInterceptor returns a gRPC unary client interceptor that:\n//\n//   - Applies policy.Timeout to each call (only if no shorter deadline is set).\n//   - Routes the call through the breaker.\n//   - Counts only server-side failures (Unavailable, DeadlineExceeded,\n//     Internal, ResourceExhausted) toward the breaker's failure tally;\n//     caller errors (InvalidArgument, NotFound, PermissionDenied,\n//     Unauthenticated) are not counted.\nfunc UnaryClientInterceptor(p *Policy, br *Breaker) grpc.UnaryClientInterceptor {\n\treturn func(\n\t\tctx context.Context,\n\t\tmethod string,\n\t\treq, reply any,\n\t\tcc *grpc.ClientConn,\n\t\tinvoker grpc.UnaryInvoker,\n\t\topts ...grpc.CallOption,\n\t) error {\n\t\t// Apply policy timeout unless a shorter deadline already exists.\n\t\tcallCtx, cancel := withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx, p.Timeout)\n\t\tdefer cancel()\n\n\t\t_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {\n\t\t\tinvErr := invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)\n\t\t\tif isCallerError(invErr) {\n\t\t\t\t// Tell gobreaker the call succeeded so caller errors don't trip it.\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn nil, invErr\n\t\t})\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {\n\tif dl, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && time.Until(dl) <= d {\n\t\treturn context.WithCancel(ctx)\n\t}\n\treturn context.WithTimeout(ctx, d)\n}\n\nfunc isCallerError(err error) bool {\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tswitch status.Code(err) {\n\tcase codes.InvalidArgument,\n\t\tcodes.NotFound,\n\t\tcodes.AlreadyExists,\n\t\tcodes.PermissionDenied,\n\t\tcodes.Unauthenticated,\n\t\tcodes.FailedPrecondition,\n\t\tcodes.OutOfRange:\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `grpc.go`**\n\nCreate `internal/resilience/grpc.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/codes\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/status\"\n)\n\n// IsGRPCCallerError reports whether err is a gRPC status that represents\n// a caller-side fault (bad input, missing resource, missing auth) rather\n// than a server-side failure. Pass this to NewBreaker via WithIsSuccessful\n// so caller errors propagate to the caller AND don't trip the breaker.\n//\n// Codes treated as caller errors:\n//   InvalidArgument, NotFound, AlreadyExists, PermissionDenied,\n//   Unauthenticated, FailedPrecondition, OutOfRange.\n//\n// Codes counted as breaker failures (caller WILL see them too):\n//   Unavailable, DeadlineExceeded, Internal, ResourceExhausted, Unknown,\n//   Aborted, Canceled, DataLoss.\nfunc IsGRPCCallerError(err error) bool {\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\treturn true // nil counts as success for IsSuccessful\n\t}\n\tswitch status.Code(err) {\n\tcase codes.InvalidArgument,\n\t\tcodes.NotFound,\n\t\tcodes.AlreadyExists,\n\t\tcodes.PermissionDenied,\n\t\tcodes.Unauthenticated,\n\t\tcodes.FailedPrecondition,\n\t\tcodes.OutOfRange:\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// UnaryClientInterceptor returns a gRPC unary client interceptor that:\n//\n//   - Applies policy.Timeout to each call (only if no shorter deadline is set).\n//   - Routes the call through the breaker.\n//   - Returns the invoker's error verbatim — the breaker decides via its\n//     IsSuccessful predicate (set in NewBreaker via WithIsSuccessful) whether\n//     the error counts toward the failure tally. Caller errors propagate\n//     to handlers AND don't trip the breaker.\nfunc UnaryClientInterceptor(p *Policy, br *Breaker) grpc.UnaryClientInterceptor {\n\treturn func(\n\t\tctx context.Context,\n\t\tmethod string,\n\t\treq, reply any,\n\t\tcc *grpc.ClientConn,\n\t\tinvoker grpc.UnaryInvoker,\n\t\topts ...grpc.CallOption,\n\t) error {\n\t\tcallCtx, cancel := withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx, p.Timeout)\n\t\tdefer cancel()\n\n\t\t_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {\n\t\t\treturn nil, invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)\n\t\t})\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {\n\tif dl, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && time.Until(dl) <= d {\n\t\treturn context.WithCancel(ctx)\n\t}\n\treturn context.WithTimeout(ctx, d)\n}\n```\n\nThe breaker constructed with `NewBreaker(policy, WithIsSuccessful(IsGRPCCallerError))` calls `IsGRPCCallerError(err)` for every result; if it returns true, the call is counted as success for breaker accounting and the error still flows back through `br.Execute` to the interceptor and the caller."
}
```

Subsequent steps refer to it as `<errfile>`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Read the existing handler to understand its dispatch shape**\n\nRead `<errfile>` and identify how it maps Go errors to HTTP status codes (likely `errors.Is`/`errors.As` switch).\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Add the ErrCircuitOpen → 503 mapping**\n\nAdd an import:\n\n```go\nimport \"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/resilience\"\n```\n\nAdd a branch in the error-mapping switch (place before the generic fallback):\n\n```go\ncase errors.Is(err, resilience.ErrCircuitOpen):\n    c.Header(\"Retry-After\", \"30\")\n    c.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, gin.H{\n        \"error\": \"service temporarily unavailable\",\n        \"code\":  \"circuit_open\",\n    })\n    return\n```\n\nIf the handler uses a custom error response shape, match the existing shape rather than `gin.H` literal.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Add a unit test**\n\nAdd to the apierror package's `*_test.go` (create one if missing):\n\n```go\nfunc TestErrorHandler_CircuitOpenReturns503(t *testing.T) {\n\tgin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)\n\tr := gin.New()\n\tr.Use(ErrorHandler())\n\tr.GET(\"/\", func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\t_ = c.Error(resilience.ErrCircuitOpen)\n\t})\n\n\treq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, \"/\", nil)\n\tw := httptest.NewRecorder()\n\tr.ServeHTTP(w, req)\n\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusServiceUnavailable {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 503\", w.Code)\n\t}\n\tif w.Header().Get(\"Retry-After\") == \"\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"missing Retry-After header\")\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Run the test**\n\n```bash\ngo test ./internal/apierror/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/apierror/\ngit commit -m \"feat(apierror): map resilience.ErrCircuitOpen to 503 with Retry-After\"\n```",
  "new_string": "## Task 10: Map `ErrCircuitOpen` → HTTP 503 in `pkg/apierror`\n\n**Depends on Task 3.**\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `pkg/apierror/apierror.go` — extend the existing `ErrorHandler()` middleware.\n- Modify: `pkg/apierror/apierror_test.go` — add the new case.\n\n**Note from grilling (2026-05-11):** the spec said `internal/apierror/`. The package actually lives at `pkg/apierror/` and an `ErrorHandler()` already exists (line 100). Its dispatch shape is **type assertion**, not `errors.Is`:\n\n```go\nif quotaErr, ok := err.(*QuotaError); ok { c.JSON(quotaErr.Code, quotaErr) }\nelse if appErr, ok := err.(*AppError); ok { c.JSON(appErr.Code, appErr) }\nelse { /* 500 fallback */ }\n```\n\nWe add an `errors.Is` branch BEFORE the type assertions so `ErrCircuitOpen` is routed to a 503 response with `Retry-After` set as a real HTTP header (not a JSON field). `pkg/apierror` will import `internal/resilience` — Go's `internal` rule allows this because `pkg/` is a sibling of `internal/` under the module root.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Update `pkg/apierror/apierror.go`**\n\nAdd to the import block:\n\n```go\nimport (\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/resilience\"\n)\n```\n\n(Adjust the module path to match `go.mod` if different.)\n\nReplace the body of `ErrorHandler` so the resilience branch fires first:\n\n```go\n// ErrorHandler is a Gin middleware that catches errors set via c.Error()\n// and returns a JSON error response.\nfunc ErrorHandler() gin.HandlerFunc {\n\treturn func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\tc.Next()\n\n\t\tif len(c.Errors) == 0 {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\terr := c.Errors.Last().Err\n\n\t\t// Resilience errors map to HTTP transport semantics, not the\n\t\t// AppError type assertions below.\n\t\tif errors.Is(err, resilience.ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\t\t// Retry-After in seconds. The breaker cooldown is a Policy\n\t\t\t// field, but pkg/apierror doesn't see the policy here, so\n\t\t\t// we use a sane default that matches the AI worker policy\n\t\t\t// default (30s). If a more precise value is needed later,\n\t\t\t// thread it via a typed error that carries the cooldown.\n\t\t\tc.Header(\"Retry-After\", strconv.Itoa(30))\n\t\t\tc.AbortWithStatusJSON(http.StatusServiceUnavailable, &AppError{\n\t\t\t\tCode:    http.StatusServiceUnavailable,\n\t\t\t\tMessage: \"Service Unavailable\",\n\t\t\t\tDetail:  \"upstream temporarily unavailable; circuit breaker open\",\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif quotaErr, ok := err.(*QuotaError); ok {\n\t\t\tc.JSON(quotaErr.Code, quotaErr)\n\t\t} else if appErr, ok := err.(*AppError); ok {\n\t\t\tc.JSON(appErr.Code, appErr)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tc.JSON(http.StatusInternalServerError, &AppError{\n\t\t\t\tCode:    http.StatusInternalServerError,\n\t\t\t\tMessage: \"Internal Server Error\",\n\t\t\t\tDetail:  err.Error(),\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Add a unit test**\n\nAdd to `pkg/apierror/apierror_test.go`:\n\n```go\nfunc TestErrorHandler_CircuitOpenReturns503WithRetryAfter(t *testing.T) {\n\tgin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)\n\tr := gin.New()\n\tr.Use(apierror.ErrorHandler())\n\tr.GET(\"/\", func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\t_ = c.Error(resilience.ErrCircuitOpen)\n\t})\n\n\treq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, \"/\", nil)\n\tw := httptest.NewRecorder()\n\tr.ServeHTTP(w, req)\n\n\tif w.Code != http.StatusServiceUnavailable {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"status = %d, want 503\", w.Code)\n\t}\n\tif w.Header().Get(\"Retry-After\") == \"\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"missing Retry-After header\")\n\t}\n}\n```\n\nPlus the matching imports.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Run the test**\n\n```bash\ngo test ./pkg/apierror/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS, including the existing tests.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add pkg/apierror/\ngit commit -m \"feat(apierror): map resilience.ErrCircuitOpen to 503 with Retry-After\"\n```"
