                            |\n|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------|------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `router.GET(\"/healthz\", ...)` (line 496)                                     | 1s     | Standalone — apply via per-route middleware list           |\n| `chatAPI := router.Group(\"/api/v1/chat\")` (line 794)                         | 30s    | Streaming chat                                             |\n| `voice := api.Group(\"/orgs/:org_id/voice-sessions\")` (line 717)              | 30s    | Voice session endpoints — applied at this nested group     |\n| `doc := kb.Group(\"/:kb_id/documents\")` (line 586)                            | 60s    | Document upload-shaped endpoints                           |\n| `src := kb.Group(\"/:kb_id/sources\")` (line 576)                              | 60s    | Source ingestion (large payloads)                          |\n| `api := router.Group(\"/api/v1\")` (line 517)                                  | 10s    | Default for everything not overridden above                |\n| `admin := router.Group(\"/api/v1/admin\")` (line 825)                          | 10s    | Admin CRUD                                                 |\n| `router.POST(\"/api/v1/billing/webhook\", ...)` (line 817)                     | 10s    | Stripe/Razorpay webhook receiver                           |\n| `router.GET(\"/webhooks/meta\", ...)` / `POST` (lines 821–822)                 | 10s    | Meta WhatsApp webhook                                      |\n| `router.GET(\"/api/v1/auth/callback\", ...)` (line 841)                        | 10s    | OAuth callback                                             |\n| `router.GET/POST(\"/api/v1/notifications/unsubscribe\", ...)` (lines 501–502)  | 10s    | Unsubscribe link handler                                   |\n\nApply order: outer `api` group gets the 10s default first (line 517 area), then the inner `voice`, `doc`, `src` groups override with their longer budgets at their respective definitions. Since `context.WithTimeout` only shortens, an inner `Deadline(60s)` on a route already wrapped in `Deadline(10s)` would actually clamp at 10s. To work around this:\n\n- Define the inner groups (`doc`, `src`, `voice`) BEFORE applying `api.Use(middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second))`, OR\n- Skip the outer-`api` Deadline and apply per-leaf-group budgets explicitly.\n\nRecommended approach: skip the outer-`api` Deadline; apply Deadline per-leaf-group:\n\n```go\n// Inside api := router.Group(\"/api/v1\"):\n//   - Each leaf endpoint group gets its own Deadline.\n//   - No outer api.Use(Deadline(...)) — would clamp inner overrides.\n\n// Standalone routes get the middleware in their per-route handler chain:\nrouter.GET(\"/healthz\", middleware.Deadline(1*time.Second), handler.HealthCheck)\nrouter.POST(\"/api/v1/billing/webhook\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), billingHandler.Webhook)\nrouter.GET(\"/webhooks/meta\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), metaWebhookHandler.VerifyWebhook)\nrouter.POST(\"/webhooks/meta\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), metaWebhookHandler.HandleEvent)\nrouter.GET(\"/api/v1/auth/callback\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), authCallback)\nrouter.GET(\"/api/v1/notifications/unsubscribe\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), notifPrefsHandler.Unsubscribe)\nrouter.POST(\"/api/v1/notifications/unsubscribe\", middleware.Deadline(10*time.Second), notifPrefsHandler.UnsubscribePost)\n\n// Inner leaf groups get their tailored budgets:\nchatAPI := router.Group(\"/api/v1/chat\")\nchatAPI.Use(middleware.Deadline(30 * time.Second))\n\nvoice := api.Group(\"/orgs/:org_id/voice-sessions\")\nvoice.Use(middleware.Deadline(30 * time.Second))\n\ndoc := kb.Group(\"/:kb_id/documents\")\ndoc.Use(middleware.Deadline(60 * time.Second))\n\nsrc := kb.Group(\"/:kb_id/sources\")\nsrc.Use(middleware.Deadline(60 * time.Second))\n```\n\nFor all OTHER endpoints under `api` (CRUD-style: `webhooks`, `leads`, `org`, `billing`, `connectors`, `llm-providers`, etc.), accept that they have NO per-route Gin deadline and rely instead on the `http.Server.WriteTimeout` (60s in step 3) as the upper bound. This is a conscious trade-off — the spec's \"default 10s on all of `/api/v1/*`\" would have required restructuring how nested groups attach Deadline, and the inheritance vs. shortening semantics make a single outer Deadline brittle. The 60s `WriteTimeout` is the safety net."
}
```

  "old_string": "## Task 12: Asynq handler timeouts (parallel-safe with Task 13)\n\n**Depends on Task 1.** Owns only files under `internal/jobs/`.\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: every file in `internal/jobs/` that defines a `ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, task *asynq.Task)` method.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Survey ProcessTask implementations**\n\n```bash\ngrep -rln 'ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, task \\*asynq.Task)' internal/jobs/\n```\n\nFor each handler file:\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Wrap each `ProcessTask` body with a per-handler timeout**\n\nFor a representative file like `internal/jobs/voice_usage.go` — at the top of the `ProcessTask` method, before any other logic:\n\n```go\nctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 30*time.Second)\ndefer cancel()\n```\n\nSizing per handler:\n\n| Handler                      | Timeout |\n|------------------------------|---------|\n| Document processing          | 5m      |\n| Voice usage aggregation      | 30s     |\n| Webhook delivery             | 30s     |\n| Email summary generation     | 2m      |\n| Default (anything else)      | 1m      |\n\nIf unsure, use 1m and leave a `// TODO(resilience): tune per workload` comment (this is the **only** acceptable TODO in this plan, because Asynq retention/timeout tuning is genuinely empirical).\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Add a unit test asserting a slow handler is bounded**\n\nPick one representative handler (e.g., `VoiceUsageHandler`) and add a test that injects a stub dependency which sleeps longer than the configured timeout, asserting the handler returns `context.DeadlineExceeded` within the budget.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run the jobs tests**\n\n```bash\ngo test ./internal/jobs/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/jobs/\ngit commit -m \"feat(jobs): apply per-handler context.WithTimeout to all Asynq processors\"\n```",
  "new_string": "## Task 12: Asynq per-task deadlines via `mux.Use` middleware (parallel-safe with Task 13)\n\n**Depends on Task 1.** Owns `internal/jobs/scheduler.go` (where the mux is built), a new `internal/jobs/deadline.go` (the middleware + budget table), and `internal/jobs/airbyte_sync.go` (one independent bug fix).\n\n**Why this shape (decided in 2026-05-11 grilling):** the original per-handler `ProcessTask` wrapping pattern would have missed 4 of the 9 handlers in `internal/jobs/`:\n\n| File | Shape | Caught by original grep? |\n|---|---|---|\n| `voice_usage.go`, `usage.go`, `cleanup.go`, `recrawl.go`, `webhook_delivery.go` | `func (h *X) ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, ...)` | ✅ |\n| `airbyte_sync.go` | `func (h *X) ProcessTask(_ context.Context, ...)` (drops ctx) | ❌ — also has independent ctx-drop bug |\n| `document_process.go`, `email_summary.go`, `send_email.go` | `func HandleX(deps) asynq.HandlerFunc` (returns a closure) | ❌ |\n\nA single `asynq.MiddlewareFunc` registered via `mux.Use(...)` wraps every handler regardless of shape, plus catches future handlers automatically. A small per-task-type budget table preserves the spec's per-handler granularity without 9 file edits.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Add `internal/jobs/deadline.go`**\n\nCreate the file:\n\n```go\npackage jobs\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/hibiken/asynq\"\n)\n\n// taskBudgets maps a task type to its per-execution deadline.\n// Keys come from the TypeXxx constants in tasks.go. Anything not in\n// the map gets defaultBudget. Tune empirically.\nvar taskBudgets = map[string]time.Duration{\n\tTypeDocumentProcess:       5 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeEmailSummary:          2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeSendEmail:             30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeVoiceUsageAggregation: 30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeUsageAggregation:      30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeWebhookDelivery:       30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeRecrawlSources:        2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeCleanupSessions:       2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeAirbyteSync:           5 * time.Minute,\n}\n\nconst defaultBudget = 1 * time.Minute\n\n// DeadlineMiddleware wraps every Asynq handler with a per-task-type\n// context.WithTimeout. Apply via mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware) so it\n// covers handlers regardless of whether they're method receivers or\n// HandlerFunc factories.\nfunc DeadlineMiddleware(next asynq.Handler) asynq.Handler {\n\treturn asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, t *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tbudget, ok := taskBudgets[t.Type()]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tbudget = defaultBudget\n\t\t}\n\t\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, budget)\n\t\tdefer cancel()\n\t\treturn next.ProcessTask(ctx, t)\n\t})\n}\n```\n\nIf any of the `TypeXxx` constants in the budget map don't exist yet (check `internal/jobs/tasks.go` and the per-handler files), use the literal string instead and leave a `// TODO: hoist to a TypeXxx constant` comment.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Wire the middleware in `scheduler.go`**\n\nAfter `mux := asynq.NewServeMux()` (around line 103) and before the `mux.Handle(...)` calls:\n\n```go\nmux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Fix `airbyte_sync.go`'s ctx drop (independent bug)**\n\nCurrently:\n\n```go\nfunc (h *AirbyteSyncHandler) ProcessTask(_ context.Context, t *asynq.Task) error {\n```\n\nChange `_` to `ctx` and thread it into the work loop. Without this, the new middleware sets a deadline on a context the handler ignores, so the budget never fires. Pattern:\n\n```go\nfunc (h *AirbyteSyncHandler) ProcessTask(ctx context.Context, t *asynq.Task) error {\n    // ... existing work, with any blocking calls accepting ctx ...\n}\n```\n\nIf the existing body has long-running calls (HTTP, DB) that don't take a context, refactor them to accept one. If that's beyond the resilience milestone scope, at minimum check `ctx.Err()` between iterations of any loop.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Add a middleware unit test**\n\nCreate `internal/jobs/deadline_test.go`:\n\n```go\npackage jobs\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/hibiken/asynq\"\n)\n\nfunc TestDeadlineMiddleware_AppliesPerTypeBudget(t *testing.T) {\n\t// Stub a slow handler that observes its ctx.\n\tvar observedErr error\n\tslow := asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, _ *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(2 * time.Second):\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\tobservedErr = ctx.Err()\n\t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\twrapped := DeadlineMiddleware(slow)\n\n\t// Use a task type with a short budget by injecting a temporary entry.\n\tconst testType = \"test:fast\"\n\ttaskBudgets[testType] = 50 * time.Millisecond\n\tt.Cleanup(func() { delete(taskBudgets, testType) })\n\n\ttask := asynq.NewTask(testType, nil)\n\tstart := time.Now()\n\terr := wrapped.ProcessTask(context.Background(), task)\n\telapsed := time.Since(start)\n\n\tif !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want DeadlineExceeded\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !errors.Is(observedErr, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"handler did not observe DeadlineExceeded; got %v\", observedErr)\n\t}\n\tif elapsed > 200*time.Millisecond {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"elapsed = %v; deadline did not fire\", elapsed)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestDeadlineMiddleware_UnknownTypeUsesDefault(t *testing.T) {\n\tcalled := false\n\th := asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, _ *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tcalled = true\n\t\tdl, ok := ctx.Deadline()\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"handler ctx had no deadline\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tif remaining := time.Until(dl); remaining > defaultBudget+time.Second {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"deadline too far away: %v\", remaining)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\n\twrapped := DeadlineMiddleware(h)\n\tif err := wrapped.ProcessTask(context.Background(), asynq.NewTask(\"test:unknown\", nil)); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"err = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !called {\n\t\tt.Error(\"handler not invoked\")\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Run the jobs tests**\n\n```bash\ngo test ./internal/jobs/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS, including the existing tests.\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/jobs/deadline.go internal/jobs/deadline_test.go internal/jobs/scheduler.go internal/jobs/airbyte_sync.go\ngit commit -m \"feat(jobs): apply per-task-type Asynq deadlines via mux middleware\"\n```"

Plan rewritten. Summary of what changed:

- **Top of file** — added a Revised note pointing at the change log.
- **Phase map** — updated to reflect Task 5 dropped and Task 12 owning more than just `internal/jobs/`.
- **Task 1 step 5** — survey grep now finds `pkg/apierror`, both Asynq handler shapes, OTel meter/tracer, and real route groups.
- **Task 3** — new `WithIsSuccessful` and `WithObservability` options on `NewBreaker`; `gobreaker.OnStateChange` wires the OTel gauge + span event; new test covers the predicate.
- **Task 4** — `grpc.go` rewritten to stop swallowing caller errors; new `IsGRPCCallerError` exported; new regression test asserts caller errors propagate to handlers verbatim.
- **Task 5** — dropped, with the original content folded behind a `<details>` for archival.
- **Task 8** — lint config simplified; widened to all production Go (blast radius confirmed = 0).
- **Task 10** — retargeted at `pkg/apierror/apierror.go` with the actual existing `ErrorHandler` shape; adds `errors.Is` branch before the type assertions; sets `Retry-After` as a real HTTP header.
- **Task 11 step 1** — `NewBreaker` call now passes `WithIsSuccessful(IsGRPCCallerError)` + `WithObservability(meter, tracer)`.
- **Task 11 step 2** — full rewrite with a real-route table anchored to actual line numbers in `cmd/api/main.go`, plus a worked-out application order that handles Gin's "context.WithTimeout only shortens" semantics.
- **Task 12** — rewritten around `mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)` + per-task-type budget map; fixes `airbyte_sync.go`'s independent ctx-drop bug; new middleware unit tests.
- **Task 13** — breaker construction in the integration tests updated to pass `WithIsSuccessful`.
- **Bottom of file** — change log table summarising all 8 resolutions.

# Resilience Layer Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add bounded-deadline + circuit-breaker resilience to the Raven Go API across the AI-worker gRPC client, `http.Server`, per-route Gin handlers, and Asynq job handlers — with unit + integration tests and a CI compliance gate.

**Architecture:** New `internal/resilience/` package owns reusable primitives (`Policy`, breaker adapter, gRPC interceptor, HTTP RoundTripper). New `internal/middleware/deadline.go` enforces per-route context deadlines. `cmd/api/main.go` wires explicit `http.Server` timeouts and the Deadline middleware. `.golangci.yml` adds `noctx` + `contextcheck` as blocking lint gates.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.22+, Gin, `google.golang.org/grpc`, `github.com/sony/gobreaker`, `github.com/hibiken/asynq`, `golangci-lint v2`.

**Spec:** [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-08-resilience-design.md`](../specs/2026-05-08-resilience-design.md)

**Branch:** `feat/resilience-layer` (worktree from `origin/main`).

**Revised:** 2026-05-11 after `/grill-with-docs` session. Eight design branches resolved against the actual codebase; see the **Revision change log** at the end of this file. The spec was authored against several assumptions that don't match the repo (apierror lives at `pkg/apierror/`, not `internal/apierror/`; `/readyz`, `/api/v1/upload/*`, `/api/v1/voice/*` route groups don't exist; Asynq handlers come in two shapes, not one). Tasks below incorporate the corrections. Task 5 (HTTP factory) is dropped entirely; remaining task numbers are unchanged for traceability.

## Parallel-execution map

Tasks group into phases. Tasks within a phase can run in parallel (disjoint file ownership). Tasks across phases are sequential.

| Phase | Tasks | Parallelizable | Notes |
|-------|-------|----------------|-------|
| 0 — Bootstrap | 1 | no | Worktree + dep + survey |
| 1 — Primitives | 2, 3, 6, 7, 8 | **yes (5 agents)** | All write disjoint files |
| 2 — Composite primitives | 4 | no | Task 5 dropped — see revision log |
| 3 — Wire | 9, 10 | **yes (2 agents)** | Depend on 2+3+4 |
| 4 — Main wiring | 11 | no | Touches cmd/api/main.go (single owner) — depends on 6, 9 |
| 5 — Asynq + integration | 12, 13 | **yes (2 agents)** | 12 owns jobs/ + scheduler.go middleware, 13 owns integration/ |
| 6 — Finish | 14, 15 | no | Sequential close-out |
```

Use absolute paths; do NOT `cd` between Bash calls.\n\n**Module path**: `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven`\n\n**Depends on**: Task 2 will create `internal/resilience/policy.go` (defines `Policy`, `Option`, `NewPolicy`, `WithBreakerThreshold`, etc.) — your code needs Policy and these options compiled. Task 2 is running in parallel; if your `go test` complains about undefined symbols from policy.go, wait briefly and retry — eventually Task 2's commit will land.\n\n**Goal**: Create `internal/resilience/breaker.go` and `internal/resilience/breaker_test.go`. The Breaker is a thin adapter over `github.com/sony/gobreaker/v2` that:\n1. Maps gobreaker's open-state errors to a typed `ErrCircuitOpen`\n2. Accepts an optional `WithIsSuccessful(fn)` predicate that classifies errors as \"doesn't count toward breaker\" while still propagating them to the caller\n3. Accepts an optional `WithObservability(meter, tracer)` that wires `gobreaker.OnStateChange` to an OTel gauge `resilience.breaker.state` (0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open) plus a span event `resilience.breaker.transition`\n\nThe `WithIsSuccessful` is the fix for the original spec's caller-error swallowing bug — see grilling change log #3.\n\n## Step 1 — Write the failing test\n\nCreate `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/resilience/breaker_test.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\nvar errCallerFault = errors.New(\"caller fault\")\n\nfunc TestBreaker_OpensAfterThreshold(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\",\n\t\tWithBreakerThreshold(3),\n\t\tWithBreakerCooldown(50*time.Millisecond),\n\t)\n\tbr := NewBreaker(p)\n\n\tfailing := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errors.New(\"boom\") }\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 3; i++ {\n\t\t_, _ = br.Execute(context.Background(), failing)\n\t}\n\n\t_, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), failing)\n\tif !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestBreaker_HalfOpenRecovers(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\",\n\t\tWithBreakerThreshold(2),\n\t\tWithBreakerCooldown(20*time.Millisecond),\n\t\tWithBreakerHalfOpenMax(1),\n\t)\n\tbr := NewBreaker(p)\n\n\tfailing := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errors.New(\"boom\") }\n\tsucc := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return \"ok\", nil }\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_, _ = br.Execute(context.Background(), failing)\n\t}\n\tif _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected ErrCircuitOpen while open, got %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\ttime.Sleep(40 * time.Millisecond)\n\n\tif _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"half-open probe err = %v, want nil\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), succ); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"post-recovery err = %v, want nil\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestBreaker_RespectsContextCancellation(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\")\n\tbr := NewBreaker(p)\n\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())\n\tcancel()\n\n\tcalled := false\n\t_, err := br.Execute(ctx, func(context.Context) (any, error) {\n\t\tcalled = true\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t})\n\tif !errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want context.Canceled\", err)\n\t}\n\tif called {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"function called despite cancelled context\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// IsSuccessful predicate: caller-classified errors propagate AND don't trip the breaker.\nfunc TestBreaker_IsSuccessfulPredicate(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithBreakerThreshold(2))\n\tbr := NewBreaker(p, WithIsSuccessful(func(err error) bool {\n\t\treturn err == nil || errors.Is(err, errCallerFault)\n\t}))\n\n\tcallerFail := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errCallerFault }\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 5; i++ {\n\t\t_, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), callerFail)\n\t\tif !errors.Is(err, errCallerFault) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"err = %v, want errCallerFault to propagate\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\trealFail := func(context.Context) (any, error) { return nil, errors.New(\"server boom\") }\n\tfor i := 0; i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_, _ = br.Execute(context.Background(), realFail)\n\t}\n\tif _, err := br.Execute(context.Background(), realFail); !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"breaker did not open after 2 server failures; err = %v\", err)\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n## Step 2 — Confirm compile failure\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer && go test ./internal/resilience/... -run TestBreaker\n```\n\nExpected: `undefined: NewBreaker`, `undefined: ErrCircuitOpen`, `undefined: WithIsSuccessful`.\n\n## Step 3 — Implement breaker.go\n\nCreate `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/resilience/breaker.go`:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sony/gobreaker/v2\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/attribute\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/metric\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/trace\"\n)\n\n// ErrCircuitOpen is returned by a Breaker when the underlying state machine\n// is Open (or in Half-Open with the probe quota exhausted). Callers should\n// surface this as HTTP 503 with Retry-After.\nvar ErrCircuitOpen = errors.New(\"resilience: circuit breaker open\")\n\n// Breaker is a thin adapter over sony/gobreaker that maps its sentinel\n// errors to ErrCircuitOpen and respects context cancellation up front.\ntype Breaker struct {\n\tcb *gobreaker.CircuitBreaker[any]\n}\n\n// BreakerOption configures NewBreaker.\ntype BreakerOption func(*breakerOpts)\n\ntype breakerOpts struct {\n\tisSuccessful func(error) bool\n\tmeter        metric.Meter\n\ttracer       trace.Tracer\n}\n\n// WithIsSuccessful classifies errors that should NOT count as breaker\n// failures (e.g. gRPC InvalidArgument is a caller bug, not a server fault).\n// The error still propagates to the caller; the breaker just ignores it.\n// The predicate must return true for nil.\nfunc WithIsSuccessful(fn func(error) bool) BreakerOption {\n\treturn func(o *breakerOpts) { o.isSuccessful = fn }\n}\n\n// WithObservability wires gobreaker's OnStateChange callback to OTel.\n// Emits a Gauge `resilience.breaker.state` (0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open)\n// labeled by policy name, plus a span event `resilience.breaker.transition`\n// on every transition. If meter or tracer is nil this option is a no-op.\nfunc WithObservability(meter metric.Meter, tracer trace.Tracer) BreakerOption {\n\treturn func(o *breakerOpts) {\n\t\tif meter != nil && tracer != nil {\n\t\t\to.meter = meter\n\t\t\to.tracer = tracer\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// NewBreaker constructs a Breaker from a validated Policy.\nfunc NewBreaker(p *Policy, opts ...BreakerOption) *Breaker {\n\to := &breakerOpts{}\n\tfor _, opt := range opts {\n\t\topt(o)\n\t}\n\n\tsettings := gobreaker.Settings{\n\t\tName:        p.Name,\n\t\tMaxRequests: p.BreakerHalfOpenMax,\n\t\tInterval:    0,\n\t\tTimeout:     p.BreakerCooldown,\n\t\tReadyToTrip: func(counts gobreaker.Counts) bool {\n\t\t\treturn counts.ConsecutiveFailures >= p.BreakerThreshold\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\tif o.isSuccessful != nil {\n\t\tsettings.IsSuccessful = o.isSuccessful\n\t}\n\tif o.meter != nil {\n\t\tvar currentState gobreaker.State\n\t\tgauge, err := o.meter.Int64ObservableGauge(\"resilience.breaker.state\",\n\t\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Circuit breaker state: 0=Closed, 1=HalfOpen, 2=Open\"))\n\t\tif err == nil {\n\t\t\t_, _ = o.meter.RegisterCallback(func(_ context.Context, obs metric.Observer) error {\n\t\t\t\tobs.ObserveInt64(gauge, int64(currentState),\n\t\t\t\t\tmetric.WithAttributes(attribute.String(\"policy_name\", p.Name)))\n\t\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t\t}, gauge)\n\t\t}\n\t\tsettings.OnStateChange = func(name string, from, to gobreaker.State) {\n\t\t\tcurrentState = to\n\t\t\t_, span := o.tracer.Start(context.Background(), \"resilience.breaker.transition\")\n\t\t\tspan.SetAttributes(\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"policy_name\", name),\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"from\", from.String()),\n\t\t\t\tattribute.String(\"to\", to.String()),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tspan.End()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn &Breaker{cb: gobreaker.NewCircuitBreaker[any](settings)}\n}\n\n// Execute runs fn through the breaker. It checks ctx cancellation first\n// to avoid charging the breaker for caller-side cancellations.\nfunc (b *Breaker) Execute(ctx context.Context, fn func(context.Context) (any, error)) (any, error) {\n\tif err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tout, err := b.cb.Execute(func() (any, error) { return fn(ctx) })\n\tswitch {\n\tcase errors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrOpenState),\n\t\terrors.Is(err, gobreaker.ErrTooManyRequests):\n\t\treturn nil, ErrCircuitOpen\n\t}\n\treturn out, err\n}\n```\n\n## Step 4 — Run tests\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer && go test ./internal/resilience/... -run TestBreaker -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS for all four. If you see \"undefined: NewPolicy\" or similar, Task 2's commit hasn't landed yet — wait 30s and retry.\n\n## Step 5 — Commit\n\n```bash\ngit -C /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer add internal/resilience/breaker.go internal/resilience/breaker_test.go\ngit -C /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer commit -m \"feat(resilience): add Breaker adapter with IsSuccessful + OTel observability\"\n```\n\nDO NOT add any `Co-Authored-By` trailer.\n\n## Output format\n\n- **Status**: DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | NEEDS_CONTEXT | BLOCKED\n- **Files created**: list with absolute paths\n- **Test result**: PASS/FAIL summary\n- **Commit SHA**: hash\n- **Concerns**: anything notable\n\nUnder 200 words.",

}

Do NOT touch other files in that package.\n\n## Step 1 — Write the failing test\n\nCreate `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/middleware/deadline_test.go`:\n\n```go\npackage middleware\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"net/http/httptest\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n)\n\nfunc TestDeadline_AppliesTimeoutToRequestContext(t *testing.T) {\n\tgin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)\n\tr := gin.New()\n\tr.Use(Deadline(50 * time.Millisecond))\n\tr.GET(\"/\", func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\tdl, ok := c.Request.Context().Deadline()\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"request ctx has no deadline\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tif remaining := time.Until(dl); remaining > 60*time.Millisecond {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"deadline too far away: %v\", remaining)\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.Status(http.StatusOK)\n\t})\n\n\treq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, \"/\", nil)\n\tw := httptest.NewRecorder()\n\tr.ServeHTTP(w, req)\n}\n\nfunc TestDeadline_PropagatesCancellation(t *testing.T) {\n\tgin.SetMode(gin.TestMode)\n\tr := gin.New()\n\tr.Use(Deadline(20 * time.Millisecond))\n\n\tvar observed error\n\tr.GET(\"/\", func(c *gin.Context) {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(100 * time.Millisecond):\n\t\tcase <-c.Request.Context().Done():\n\t\t\tobserved = c.Request.Context().Err()\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.Status(http.StatusOK)\n\t})\n\n\treq := httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, \"/\", nil)\n\tw := httptest.NewRecorder()\n\tr.ServeHTTP(w, req)\n\n\tif !errors.Is(observed, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"ctx err = %v, want DeadlineExceeded\", observed)\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n## Step 2 — Confirm compile failure\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer && go test ./internal/middleware/... -run TestDeadline\n```\n\nExpected: `undefined: Deadline`.\n\n## Step 3 — Implement deadline.go\n\nCreate `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/middleware/deadline.go`:\n\n```go\npackage middleware\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/gin-gonic/gin\"\n)\n\n// Deadline returns a Gin middleware that wraps the request context\n// in context.WithTimeout(d). === grpc.go ===
package resilience

import (
	"context"
	"time"

	"google.golang.org/grpc"
	"google.golang.org/grpc/codes"
	"google.golang.org/grpc/status"
)

// UnaryClientInterceptor returns a gRPC unary client interceptor that:
//
//   - Applies policy.Timeout to each call (only if no shorter deadline is set).
//   - Routes the call through the breaker.
//   - Counts only server-side failures (Unavailable, DeadlineExceeded,
//     Internal, ResourceExhausted) toward the breaker's failure tally;
//     caller errors (InvalidArgument, NotFound, PermissionDenied,
//     Unauthenticated) are not counted.
func UnaryClientInterceptor(p *Policy, br *Breaker) grpc.UnaryClientInterceptor {
	return func(
		ctx context.Context,
		method string,
		req, reply any,
		cc *grpc.ClientConn,
		invoker grpc.UnaryInvoker,
		opts ...grpc.CallOption,
	) error {
		// Apply policy timeout unless a shorter deadline already exists.
		callCtx, cancel := withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx, p.Timeout)
		defer cancel()

		_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {
			invErr := invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)
			if isCallerError(invErr) {
				// Tell gobreaker the call succeeded so caller errors don't trip it.
				return nil, nil
			}
			return nil, invErr
		})
		return err
	}
}

func withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {
	if dl, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && time.Until(dl) <= d {
		return context.WithCancel(ctx)
	}
	return context.WithTimeout(ctx, d)
}

func isCallerError(err error) bool {
	if err == nil {
		return false
	}
	switch status.Code(err) {
	case codes.InvalidArgument,
		codes.NotFound,
		codes.AlreadyExists,
		codes.PermissionDenied,
		codes.Unauthenticated,
		codes.FailedPrecondition,
		codes.OutOfRange:
		return true
	}
	return false
}

{
  "description": "Task 4: Fix grpc.go caller-error",
  "subagent_type": "general-purpose",
  "prompt": "You are implementing the **revised Task 4** from the Raven resilience-layer plan. This is a CORRECTION pass — the file already exists and has a known bug.\n\n**Worktree**: `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer`. Use absolute paths; do NOT `cd` between Bash calls.\n**Module path**: `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven`\n\n**The bug being fixed** (caught during /grill-with-docs session): `internal/resilience/grpc.go` currently does:\n\n```go\n_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {\n    invErr := invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)\n    if isCallerError(invErr) {\n        return nil, nil  // ← SILENTLY SWALLOWS CALLER ERROR\n    }\n    return nil, invErr\n})\nreturn err  // ← err is now nil for caller errors → handlers deref nil reply → panic\n```\n\nWhen the AI worker returns `codes.InvalidArgument` (or any other caller-side gRPC code), the interceptor returns `nil` to the caller. Handlers think the call succeeded, deref the nil reply, panic.\n\n**The fix**: use `gobreaker.Settings.IsSuccessful` (already exposed via `WithIsSuccessful` option on `NewBreaker` in the recently-updated `breaker.go`) so the breaker classifies caller errors as \"doesn't count toward failure\" while the error still propagates to the caller. Export `IsGRPCCallerError` so main.go can pass it to `NewBreaker`.\n\n## Steps\n\n### Step 1 — Replace `internal/resilience/grpc.go`\n\nOverwrite `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/resilience/grpc.go` with:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/codes\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/status\"\n)\n\n// IsGRPCCallerError reports whether err is a gRPC status that represents\n// a caller-side fault (bad input, missing resource, missing auth) rather\n// than a server-side failure. Pass this to NewBreaker via WithIsSuccessful\n// so caller errors propagate to the caller AND don't trip the breaker.\n//\n// Returns true for nil so it satisfies the IsSuccessful contract (success\n// counts as success).\n//\n// Codes treated as caller errors (success-equivalent for breaker accounting):\n//\n//\tInvalidArgument, NotFound, AlreadyExists, PermissionDenied,\n//\tUnauthenticated, FailedPrecondition, OutOfRange.\n//\n// Codes counted as breaker failures (caller still sees them):\n//\n//\tUnavailable, DeadlineExceeded, Internal, ResourceExhausted, Unknown,\n//\tAborted, Canceled, DataLoss.\nfunc IsGRPCCallerError(err error) bool {\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\tswitch status.Code(err) {\n\tcase codes.InvalidArgument,\n\t\tcodes.NotFound,\n\t\tcodes.AlreadyExists,\n\t\tcodes.PermissionDenied,\n\t\tcodes.Unauthenticated,\n\t\tcodes.FailedPrecondition,\n\t\tcodes.OutOfRange:\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// UnaryClientInterceptor returns a gRPC unary client interceptor that:\n//\n//   - Applies policy.Timeout to each call (only if no shorter deadline is set).\n//   - Routes the call through the breaker.\n//   - Returns the invoker's error verbatim. Whether the error counts toward\n//     the breaker's failure tally is decided by the breaker's IsSuccessful\n//     predicate (set via NewBreaker(p, WithIsSuccessful(...))).\nfunc UnaryClientInterceptor(p *Policy, br *Breaker) grpc.UnaryClientInterceptor {\n\treturn func(\n\t\tctx context.Context,\n\t\tmethod string,\n\t\treq, reply any,\n\t\tcc *grpc.ClientConn,\n\t\tinvoker grpc.UnaryInvoker,\n\t\topts ...grpc.CallOption,\n\t) error {\n\t\tcallCtx, cancel := withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx, p.Timeout)\n\t\tdefer cancel()\n\n\t\t_, err := br.Execute(callCtx, func(c context.Context) (any, error) {\n\t\t\treturn nil, invoker(c, method, req, reply, cc, opts...)\n\t\t})\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc withTimeoutIfShorter(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) (context.Context, context.CancelFunc) {\n\tif dl, ok := ctx.Deadline(); ok && time.Until(dl) <= d {\n\t\treturn context.WithCancel(ctx)\n\t}\n\treturn context.WithTimeout(ctx, d)\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2 — Update `internal/resilience/grpc_test.go`\n\nRead the existing file first (it has tests using the old breaker constructor that lacked WithIsSuccessful). Replace its contents with:\n\n```go\npackage resilience\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/codes\"\n\t\"google.golang.org/grpc/status\"\n)\n\ntype fakeInvoker struct {\n\tcalls int\n\terr   error\n\tdelay time.Duration\n}\n\nfunc (f *fakeInvoker) invoke(ctx context.Context, _ string, _, _ any, _ *grpc.ClientConn, _ ...grpc.CallOption) error {\n\tf.calls++\n\tif f.delay > 0 {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(f.delay):\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn f.err\n}\n\nfunc newGRPCBreaker(p *Policy) *Breaker {\n\treturn NewBreaker(p, WithIsSuccessful(IsGRPCCallerError))\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_AppliesTimeout(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithTimeout(20*time.Millisecond))\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, newGRPCBreaker(p))\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{delay: 100 * time.Millisecond}\n\terr := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\n\tif !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want context.DeadlineExceeded\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_OpensBreakerOnUnavailable(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\",\n\t\tWithTimeout(1*time.Second),\n\t\tWithBreakerThreshold(2),\n\t\tWithBreakerCooldown(100*time.Millisecond),\n\t)\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.Unavailable, \"down\")}\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 2; i++ {\n\t\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\t}\n\tpreCalls := inv.calls\n\terr := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif !errors.Is(err, ErrCircuitOpen) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want ErrCircuitOpen\", err)\n\t}\n\tif inv.calls != preCalls {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"invoker called %d times after open; want 0\", inv.calls-preCalls)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsDoNotTrip(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\", WithBreakerThreshold(2))\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, \"bad\")}\n\n\tfor i := 0; i < 5; i++ {\n\t\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\t}\n\n\tpreCalls := inv.calls\n\t_ = icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif inv.calls == preCalls {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"breaker tripped on caller errors\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// Regression: caller errors must propagate to the caller verbatim.\n// Pre-fix the interceptor returned nil for caller-classified errors, which\n// caused handlers to deref nil reply and panic.\nfunc TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsPropagate(t *testing.T) {\n\tp, _ := NewPolicy(\"svc\")\n\tbr := newGRPCBreaker(p)\n\ticpt := UnaryClientInterceptor(p, br)\n\n\twant := status.Error(codes.InvalidArgument, \"bad input\")\n\tinv := &fakeInvoker{err: want}\n\n\tgot := icpt(context.Background(), \"/svc/Method\", nil, nil, nil, inv.invoke)\n\tif got == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"interceptor returned nil; want the caller error to propagate\")\n\t}\n\tif status.Code(got) != codes.InvalidArgument {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"propagated code = %v, want InvalidArgument\", status.Code(got))\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3 — Run the tests\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer && go test ./internal/resilience/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS for all tests including the new `TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsPropagate`.\n\n### Step 4 — Lint check\n\nManually run lint (the pre-commit hook may OOM in the sandbox). Use absolute paths; do NOT `cd` between Bash calls.\n**Module path**: `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven`\n\n**Background**: PR #411 already added per-handler `context.WithTimeout` to 5 of the 9 Asynq handlers (cleanup, recrawl, usage, voice_usage, webhook_delivery). FOUR handlers are uncovered:\n- `internal/jobs/airbyte_sync.go` — uses `_ context.Context` (drops the parameter entirely)\n- `internal/jobs/document_process.go` — `HandleX(...) asynq.HandlerFunc` shape\n- `internal/jobs/email_summary.go` — same closure shape\n- `internal/jobs/send_email.go` — same closure shape\n\n**The fix** (decided in /grill-with-docs session): replace per-handler wrapping with a single `mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)` registered in `scheduler.go`. The middleware reads a per-task-type budget from a map. This catches ALL handlers regardless of shape, plus future ones automatically. ALSO fix `airbyte_sync.go` to honour ctx instead of dropping it.\n\n## Steps\n\n### Step 1 — Read existing context\n\nRead these files in full BEFORE editing:\n- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/jobs/scheduler.go` — to see how mux is built\n- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/jobs/tasks.go` — to find the TypeXxx constants\n- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/jobs/airbyte_sync.go` — to understand the existing handler\n\nTake note of the actual TypeXxx constant names. Common patterns: `TypeRecrawlSources`, `TypeCleanupSessions`, `TypeUsageAggregation`, `TypeVoiceUsageAggregation`, `TypeWebhookDelivery`, `TypeDocumentProcess`, `TypeEmailSummary`, `TypeSendEmail`, `TypeAirbyteSync`. If any are missing or named differently, adjust the budget map accordingly.\n\n### Step 2 — Create `internal/jobs/deadline.go`\n\nCreate `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/jobs/deadline.go`:\n\n```go\npackage jobs\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/hibiken/asynq\"\n)\n\n// taskBudgets maps a task type to its per-execution deadline.\n// Keys come from the TypeXxx constants in tasks.go (or per-file constants).\n// Anything not in the map gets defaultBudget.\nvar taskBudgets = map[string]time.Duration{\n\tTypeDocumentProcess:       5 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeEmailSummary:          2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeSendEmail:             30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeVoiceUsageAggregation: 30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeUsageAggregation:      30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeWebhookDelivery:       30 * time.Second,\n\tTypeRecrawlSources:        2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeCleanupSessions:       2 * time.Minute,\n\tTypeAirbyteSync:           5 * time.Minute,\n}\n\nconst defaultBudget = 1 * time.Minute\n\n// DeadlineMiddleware wraps every Asynq handler with a per-task-type\n// context.WithTimeout. Apply via mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware) so it\n// covers handlers regardless of whether they're method receivers or\n// HandlerFunc factories.\nfunc DeadlineMiddleware(next asynq.Handler) asynq.Handler {\n\treturn asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, t *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tbudget, ok := taskBudgets[t.Type()]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tbudget = defaultBudget\n\t\t}\n\t\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, budget)\n\t\tdefer cancel()\n\t\treturn next.ProcessTask(ctx, t)\n\t})\n}\n```\n\nIf any TypeXxx constant doesn't exist, use the literal task type string the handler uses (look at how the handler is registered in `scheduler.go` mux.Handle calls). Match the convention exactly.\n\n### Step 3 — Wire `mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)` in scheduler.go\n\nEdit `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer/internal/jobs/scheduler.go`. Find `mux := asynq.NewServeMux()` and immediately after it (BEFORE the `mux.Handle(...)` calls) add:\n\n```go\n\tmux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)\n```\n\n### Step 4 — Remove the now-redundant per-handler `context.WithTimeout` calls\n\nEach of these files has a `ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, X*time.Y); defer cancel()` at the top of `ProcessTask`. The middleware now provides the deadline; remove the per-handler wrapping (delete the two lines + adjust the time/imports if no longer used). Files:\n- `internal/jobs/cleanup.go`\n- `internal/jobs/recrawl.go`\n- `internal/jobs/usage.go`\n- `internal/jobs/voice_usage.go`\n- `internal/jobs/webhook_delivery.go`\n\nAfter removing the per-handler timeout block, run `goimports` mentally — if `time` is no longer used in the file, remove the import.\n\n### Step 5 — Fix `internal/jobs/airbyte_sync.go`\n\nCurrently: `func (h *AirbyteSyncHandler) ProcessTask(_ context.Context, t *asynq.Task) error {`\n\nChange `_ context.Context` to `ctx context.Context` and use ctx for any cancellable work in the body. If the body has long-running calls that don't currently take a context (HTTP, DB), pass `ctx` to them where signatures allow. At minimum, check `ctx.Err()` between iterations of any loop. Read the entire file before deciding what minimum-viable threading looks like.\n\n### Step 6 — Add `internal/jobs/deadline_test.go`\n\n```go\npackage jobs\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/hibiken/asynq\"\n)\n\nfunc TestDeadlineMiddleware_AppliesPerTypeBudget(t *testing.T) {\n\tvar observedErr error\n\tslow := asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, _ *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-time.After(2 * time.Second):\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\tobservedErr = ctx.Err()\n\t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\twrapped := DeadlineMiddleware(slow)\n\n\tconst testType = \"test:fast\"\n\ttaskBudgets[testType] = 50 * time.Millisecond\n\tt.Cleanup(func() { delete(taskBudgets, testType) })\n\n\ttask := asynq.NewTask(testType, nil)\n\tstart := time.Now()\n\terr := wrapped.ProcessTask(context.Background(), task)\n\telapsed := time.Since(start)\n\n\tif !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"err = %v, want DeadlineExceeded\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !errors.Is(observedErr, context.DeadlineExceeded) {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"handler did not observe DeadlineExceeded; got %v\", observedErr)\n\t}\n\tif elapsed > 200*time.Millisecond {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"elapsed = %v; deadline did not fire\", elapsed)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestDeadlineMiddleware_UnknownTypeUsesDefault(t *testing.T) {\n\tcalled := false\n\th := asynq.HandlerFunc(func(ctx context.Context, _ *asynq.Task) error {\n\t\tcalled = true\n\t\tdl, ok := ctx.Deadline()\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"handler ctx had no deadline\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tif remaining := time.Until(dl); remaining > defaultBudget+time.Second {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"deadline too far away: %v\", remaining)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\n\twrapped := DeadlineMiddleware(h)\n\tif err := wrapped.ProcessTask(context.Background(), asynq.NewTask(\"test:unknown\", nil)); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"err = %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !called {\n\t\tt.Error(\"handler not invoked\")\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Step 7 — Run the jobs tests\n\n```bash\ncd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.worktrees/feat-resilience-layer && go test ./internal/jobs/... -v\n```\n\nExpected: PASS, including all existing tests. - `cmd/worker/main.go` — added `srv.Mux().Use(jobs.DeadlineMiddleware)` on the worker mux (the on-demand handlers `DocumentProcess`, `EmailSummary`, `SendEmail`, `WebhookDelivery` register here, NOT on the scheduler mux — this discovery was load-bearing; without this second `Use`, four of the five "uncovered" handlers would have stayed uncovered)

**Test result:** all `internal/jobs` tests PASS (count=1), including the two new `TestDeadlineMiddleware_*` cases. Existing `timeout_test.go` cases (which assert generic `context.WithTimeout` behaviour, not handler internals) all still pass. Full `go build ./...` succeeds.

3. Discovered the worker mux is a SECOND mux distinct from the scheduler mux — added `Use()` in both places. Without that, `mux.Use` in scheduler.go alone would have left the on-demand handlers uncovered (the original problem).

{
  "command": "gh pr create --base main --head fix/resilience-grilling-corrections --title \"fix(resilience): apply grill-with-docs corrections to PR #411\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nFollow-up to #411 applying eight corrections surfaced during a `/grill-with-docs` review session against the merged resilience layer:\n\n- **fix(grpc):** UnaryClientInterceptor was silently swallowing caller-side gRPC errors (`InvalidArgument`, `NotFound`, etc.) — handlers received `nil` instead of the actual error and would deref a nil reply. Refactored to return invoker error verbatim and let the breaker classify via `gobreaker.Settings.IsSuccessful`. New regression test `TestUnaryClientInterceptor_CallerErrorsPropagate`.\n- **feat(resilience):** New `WithIsSuccessful(fn)` and `WithObservability(meter, tracer)` options on `NewBreaker`. Exported `IsGRPCCallerError` predicate. OTel gauge `resilience.breaker.state` (0/1/2) + span event `resilience.breaker.transition` wired via `gobreaker.OnStateChange`.\n- **feat(api):** `cmd/api/main.go` constructs `aiBreaker` with both new options.\n- **refactor(jobs):** Replaced per-handler `context.WithTimeout` (covered only 5 of 9 handlers) with a single `mux.Use(DeadlineMiddleware)` wired in both `internal/jobs/scheduler.go` AND `cmd/worker/main.go` (a separate mux discovered during implementation). Per-task-type budget map covers all current and future handlers. ## Done.

**PR #490 — `fix(resilience): apply grill-with-docs corrections to PR #411`**
https://github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/pull/490 — auto-merge (squash) enqueued.

### What happened

The plan's foundational work was already merged via PR #411 before today. The bootstrap agent flagged this immediately — pivoted from "implement the 15-task plan" to "ship the grilling corrections as a follow-up PR."

### Six commits on `fix/resilience-grilling-corrections`

| SHA | Commit |
|---|---|
| `b9de9613` | chore(resilience): bootstrap branch with spec, plan, and gobreaker dep |
| `51446bbb` | ci(lint): enable noctx and contextcheck repo-wide for resilience compliance gate |
| `e1538fb1` | feat(resilience): add Breaker adapter with IsSuccessful + OTel observability |
| `0643d12b` | fix(resilience): propagate caller-side gRPC errors instead of swallowing |
| `c83ae1f6` | refactor(jobs): apply per-task-type Asynq deadlines via mux.Use middleware |
| `95e7bdc8` | feat(api): wire WithIsSuccessful + WithObservability into AI worker breaker |

### Real bugs fixed

1. **gRPC interceptor swallowed caller errors** — handlers received `nil` for `InvalidArgument`/`NotFound`/etc. and would deref nil reply. Now propagates verbatim; breaker classifies via `IsSuccessful` predicate. Regression test added.
2. **4 of 9 Asynq handlers had no deadline** — agent discovered a *second* mux in `cmd/worker/main.go` distinct from the scheduler mux; wired middleware in both. `airbyte_sync.go` ctx-drop fixed so the deadline actually fires.
3. **OTel observability missing** — gauge `resilience.breaker.state` + span event `resilience.breaker.transition` now wired via `gobreaker.OnStateChange`.
4. **Lint exclusions removed** — repo-wide `noctx` + `contextcheck` confirmed clean.

### Deferred to follow-ups (not in this PR)

- Drop the unused HTTP factory in `internal/resilience/http.go`
- Extend `Deadline` middleware to admin/src/doc groups + standalone webhook handlers in main.go

### Pre-existing issue spotted

`TestRLS_MigrationVersion_AllApplied` hardcodes 37 migrations but the codebase is at 39 since the Ollama PR (#480). On `main`, not introduced here. Flagged in the PR body — warrants its own fix.

### Verification before push

- `go test ./internal/resilience/...` PASS (18 tests)
- `go test ./internal/jobs/...` PASS
- `go build ./...` clean
- `golangci-lint run` clean across all touched packages

## Read on that failure

**Trivy is flagging pre-existing dep CVEs, not your PR's changes.** The findings:

| Package | File | CVE | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `langchain-core` 1.3.0 | `ai-worker/uv.lock` | CVE-2026-44843 (unsafe deserialization) | 1.3.3 |
| `urllib3` 2.6.3 | `ai-worker/uv.lock` | CVE-2026-44431, -44432 | 2.7.0 |
| `next` 16.2.3 | `landing/package-lock.json` | 7 HIGHs (middleware bypass × 4, SSRF, DoS × 2) | 16.2.5 / 16.2.6 |

PR #495 touches only `cmd/api/main.go` — none of these are reachable by the diff. Same failure would land on any open PR until the deps are bumped.

## Current state

- **PR #494** → already **MERGED** (admin override / Trivy isn't a required check)
- **PR #495** → re-running CI right now (most checks queued/in-progress); Trivy hasn't fired yet on this run

## Options

1. **Let it ride** — once Trivy fails again, decide whether to override. Same path PR #494 took.
2. **Bump the deps in a separate PR first** — three small dependabot-style PRs (langchain-core, urllib3, next), Trivy clears, then #495 auto-merges on its next CI run.
3. **Bump deps directly on #495** — fastest but scope-creeps the PR.

Recommend **(2)**: file three independent dep bumps. Want me to spin those up in parallel?
